mr president, the presidencies of the council of the european union are not a separate event, isolated from the chronological context of previous events. the german presidency was set in the context of the british administration and the creation of the single currency. it is therefore impossible to assess the presidency which has just expired without taking account of the low profile of the one that preceded it and the hopes raised by the birth of the euro, which then fell into nothing and remained on paper. the treaty of amsterdam had not solved a problem of the functioning of the institutions and in the field of foreign policy the only step forward had been to appoint mr cushnahan, who, however, was not yet in office during the kosovo crisis which, without waiting for delays and the continuing absence of europe, erupted like a plague and renewed the crimes of ethnic cleansing which had already plagued bosnia. the german presidency therefore found itself without the appropriate instruments at its disposal and the atlantic alliance was the only suitable support to counter milosevic's policy. the weight of the union, despite the ambiguous movements of some governments, was irrelevant for military action and europe was not perceived as having an independent will, even in respect of alliances. has progress been made with the cologne european council? perhaps, in terms of stated intentions, yes, but it seems to us that everything is still very nebulous before we can say by what date the union will be able to be an autonomous political entity with its own foreign and security policy. the responsibility of governments for the delay in the implementation of political union is enormous and we do not see any change in the pace of the german presidency. the only merit can be that we have at least put the question of common defence on the agenda for cologne. another issue on which the german presidency has not made any progress is that of employment. after having flagged at every opportunity during the german election campaign that with the socialists coming to power everything would change and a virtuous path would be started which would create many jobs, reversing the negative trend that europe has been experiencing for at least a decade, no initiative has been taken in this direction and no proposal has been discussed for the development of the european economy. without a concerted economic policy aimed at development, it will be difficult to combat unemployment and create the conditions which can halt the decline of the euro. if the german presidency did not do so, how can the finnish presidency do so today? and how can we seriously tackle unemployment as we get closer and closer to the date of enlargement? community policy, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union, economic and monetary union these are the priorities which no presidency can ignore. but these are problems that the presidencies talk about, pass by and do not implement, and the european union is languishing and the citizens continue to ask us for fewer words and a few facts.
the council. before i embark on a trip to turkey, let me briefly touch on what has been said here. i would like to thank all those who have praised us and supported us and to respond to some of the criticisms. we have never promised - and it would be out of this world - that in this six months we would have been able to solve all the problems in the european union that i had long wanted to see solved. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european company law is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. i am glad that this has been achieved. i see this as an important step forward. but here the critics got a lot wrong, and sometimes the logic got in the way. the pds colleague who criticised us for ignoring the french situation and the aims of french policy, but also strongly criticised the lack of results in the agricultural market compromise at the berlin summit, i can only say, madam president, that these are somehow interrelated and you should take a closer look at this, so that you do not end up in a tangle of contradictions with yourself. i am sure that in the coming weeks and months you will see that there is a very close link here. i would like to say to those - and this is an important point - who are talking about the militarization of the european union, that you are living in your illusions. this would mean that europe would not be able to determine its own destiny, that is to say, to follow its own path in the field of security and foreign policy. i have experienced in the kosovo war what it means to put europe on the scale, also in terms of what political options there are, what possibilities there are for pursuing a preventive foreign policy, a preventive peace policy, for pursuing a solution to a conflict when the conflict has escalated. i can only say that this europe must come together, also in security and foreign policy, otherwise decisions will be taken elsewhere. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. in reality, however, these decisions are made under very different conditions and in very different places. this is precisely what i do not want and i think this is a wrong development. (applause from the centre) with the euro, we have - and we should not hide - different views of europe. yes, there are, obviously. i am a firm integrationist, and i want a europe which is capable of taking political action, because i am convinced of two things: there is no other way. the first reason is that in the age of globalisation, the european nation state, what we value and want to preserve in the european nation state, can only be preserved if this europe is united, because the scale of the classical european nation state, in the conditions of globalisation, will only be able to guarantee peace, security, prosperity and democracy for its citizens if it is european in character, so that we have a european subject, a european union capable of action. the second reason is that the kosovo war has made this clear again, and perhaps the different historical experiences play a role here, particularly in central europe, there is only one security on this continent: we cannot allow nationalism, racism, and a crude form of fascism, to exist on this continent. since 1992, everything has been done to persuade milosevic to abandon this policy. i have lived through the final phase of the war from rambouillet to the beginning of the military action, and i have not met anyone on the western side, including the united states, who has acted as a warmonger, but there has been a struggle for compromise until the proverbial last second. the only one who did not want to compromise was milosevic, the government in belgrade. i myself have personally witnessed a two-and-a-half-hour discussion with milosevic - an hour of it in private. should we have just sat back? should we have said that we would like to help but we cannot help, that it is against our pacifist principles, that we have no un security council resolution? the un is now stronger after this conflict, and this is one of the effects of europe's role in this conflict. the security council is stronger today than it was in bosnia when it emerged from the conflict weakened. i think we should recognize... president prodi, this is your third meeting with this parliament, a parliament which has changed a great deal but where there is an institutional continuity which we must all respect. we know the directives you intend to give to the commission, which you have completed and clarified today with regard to the reform of the institution, and you know the orientation and also the political concerns of the european parliament. i'm going to do three very quick reflections. the first: we share the objective of reviving economic union through political europe. this requires a strong synergy between the commission and parliament, a parliament which has the democratic representation of the european citizens and which must therefore make a fundamental contribution to the reform of the european institutions. this is the house in which, as you have said on other occasions, we must seek that european soul which is dominant for the future of the continent. but if we are to restore trust between the institutions and the people of europe, we think that a relationship of loyal and strong cooperation between the european institutions, and therefore between parliament and the commission, must be established. the second reflection concerns the objections and the debate which we shall resume in september. the objections, i believe, are primarily aimed at verifying the compatibility between the choices he made in the formation of the team he presented today and the general guidelines he presented to this parliament. we fully support a strategy based on the conviction that it is essential to respect the stability pact and we share the view that the fundamental question today is to make europe a more competitive system in order to get back on the road to development, also in the fight against unemployment. we think, however, that it is also appropriate to stress the reasons why there has recently been talk of an employment pact and the need to pay close attention to the regional imbalances that still exist in europe. without a european strategy in this respect, there is a risk that an economy such as ours will tend to concentrate its resources in the strongest areas, leaving the weakest on the margins, even though some of these have made considerable progress in recent times. the third and final point concerns the speech which president martens made in this parliament on behalf of the european people during the debate on the political guidelines. he expressed strong support for his candidacy and called for the commission to tackle some of the problems that were hampering its work. i believe, however, that, however particular the constitutional character of the commission/parliament relations, the concerns raised about the political balance that exists in president, i also warmly congratulate you on your election. i'm very happy to be able to start working with you. in accordance with parliament's wishes, i shall now present the main points of finland's presidency programme. i shall deal with your request in particular with the situation in the western balkans and the death sentence against the kurdish leader, öcalan. but first, a few words on the development of the union in general. in his speech, mr prodi drew attention to the way in which the european union's institutions have lagged behind the pace of change in the world around us. i fully agree with the commission's nominee. it is not just the commission that needs to be reformed, but the whole of the european union. it is not a one-off revolution, nor is it the work of one single institution, but a continuous process. we must remember that it is the capacity to grow, to expand and to meet ever new challenges that has made the union both unique and sustainable. today, as we begin our cooperation, we have the opportunity to do so from a kind of clean slate. the new parliament has now begun its work and the appointment of the new commission is under way. we finns have for the first time assumed the presidency of the council. the recent entry into force of the amsterdam treaty provides us with a good basis for developing ever closer cooperation between parliament, the commission and the council. we now have a great opportunity to start to renew the old ways of doing things. for my part, i would like to stress that the work of the council must also be developed. it is paradoxical that, as the union's importance increases, the cooperation of its governments in the council is threatened by a fragmentation and sectoralisation of the exercise of power. the council, like the union as a whole, must concentrate on the essential. the council is expected to provide political leadership that adds real value to the lives of citizens. finland intends to initiate and implement a number of projects to develop the work of the council during its presidency. we are seeking to make the council's work more transparent and effective, and we consider that greater transparency is a necessary precondition for the whole union to be acceptable to the public. we also want the general affairs council to take up its proper role in directing the council's work. we must do everything we can to ensure that the union is and is seen as one: it must have a common will, a common policy and a common voice. the new president, mr prodi, said that the keywords for the new commission were transparency, accountability and efficiency. i am very pleased to see that the new commission's approach corresponds exactly to the main priorities of the finnish presidency. finland has taken over the presidency of the council at a time when the union's external action capacity is under exceptional pressure. the war in the western balkans has caused an unimaginable amount of human suffering and material damage. the european union must have the political and economic capacity to contribute to the development of the western balkans in order to give it the chance to establish democracy and stability. during its presidency, finland will pay particular attention to the efficiency and consistency of the union's external action. the post-kosovo crisis and the stabilisation of the western balkans are the most important test for us. the european union's general affairs council on monday stressed that the union would continue to help the serbian people who are suffering from the destructive policies of their leaders. in the next stage of the lifting of sanctions against the federal republic of yugoslavia, we will take into account that the government in belgrade and the people of yugoslavia are two different things. progress towards democratization. we agreed on monday that the first thing to be done is to lift the restrictions on the population, such as the flight ban and the avoidance of contact in the sports sector. we stressed the need to free kosovo and montenegro quickly from oil import sanctions and other sanctions. we also consider it important to support all those in the federal republic of yugoslavia who are committed to democratic values. we should then define the means and methods to facilitate the situation of such operators. this includes, for example, the supply of electricity and crude oil. we asked the commission and the competent bodies to make proposals without delay. we also confirmed in the general affairs council the decision of the heads of state or government on the location of the agency for reconstruction and the decision on the south-east european stability pact summit in sarajevo. the kosovo crisis can be the dividing line from which the different forms of external action of the union are decisively steered towards a common goal. e union's external relations toolbox needs to be strengthened to improve its effectiveness, our capacity to anticipate crises needs to be improved and our post-crisis preparedness needs to be strengthened. we discussed this at length on monday at the open session of the general affairs council. we were all very much in agreement about developing this sector on a fast timetable. the european union is best placed to contribute to regional stability through a consistent and determined policy. i would like us to remember, however, that the union is far from being solely responsible for the post-crisis management of kosovo. we work closely with other countries and international organisations. it is precisely the further development and deepening of cooperation with the united nations, the osce and the council of europe that is essential. on several occasions, members of parliament have stressed the importance of the union as a motor and guarantor of peaceful and balanced development on our continent. this is what the next enlargement is all about. the european union is gradually becoming a pan-european union. in at least one respect, the enlargement to follow is different from all previous ones: the union's own cooperation has been deepened to a level which places very high demands on all applicants to achieve. i am convinced, however, that these requirements are good for both the applicant countries and the union. the fulfilment of the criteria strengthens the social and economic development of the applicant countries, and this has also been noted in the applicant countries. enlargement is a process that must be moved forward all the time and must not be jeopardised by empty promises. the finnish presidency will continue the accession negotiations with determination, with the aim of completing as many negotiating tables as possible and starting new ones. the helsinki european council is to decide, on the basis of a commission report, on the inclusion of the new applicant countries in the accession negotiations. at the same time, we considered the development of relations with european countries and the long-term prospects for enlargement. enlargement will require adaptation of the institutional system of the union: we have already begun preparations for a new intergovernmental conference, and we intend to draw up a report on this subject for the helsinki european council. parliament's views are of paramount importance in this reform and i have noted with satisfaction that our views are very similar on many issues. we cannot forget turkey in the enlargement process. turkey is an important partner for the union. it is involved in the enlargement process and has its own strategy. turkey's prospects for membership are influenced by its progress in democratic reforms and the development of human rights, especially minority rights. in this context, i would like to emphasise that the union is opposed to the death penalty everywhere and in all cases. the abolition of the death penalty is a common objective of the european union. finland will be monitoring the situation as chair. the execution of the verdict against the kurdish leader öcalan would be a regrettable turn in turkish politics. turkey is a member of the council of europe and has therefore joined the objective of abolishing the death penalty. the country has not carried out any executions since 1984. it is desirable that turkey continue to suspend the execution of the sentence until the death penalty is abolished. it is hoped that the turkish parliament will use its opportunity to overturn the sentence. turkey has expressed its desire to join the european union. the abstention from the death penalty is part of the common values of the union. mr president, i shall not go through the entire programme of the presidency at this point. my visit to the committee in september will provide an opportunity to continue the debate on external relations. the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force, and it is also a political force, and it is a political force, and it is a political force. the european union is actively promoting the peace process, both politically and economically. i will be travelling to the region in early august to meet the main actors and to convey the union's ideas on promoting the peace process. whether we are talking about enlargement or conflict prevention, we can be sure of one thing: we can only help others if our own well-being is on a firm footing. knowledge and skills are fundamental to our own well-being and competitiveness, that of the member states and the union as a whole. the information society offers new opportunities for citizens, businesses and the public sector. in the coming months, we will be exploring how the information society can be used to improve competitiveness. at the same time, we must ensure that development does not widen the gap between different groups in society. we have not yet fully exploited the opportunities that monetary union and the single market offer us. we can only create new jobs if our economy is in good shape. the improvement in employment is an indication that we are moving in the right direction. we will continue to work on gender equality, social and ecological sustainability, youth and citizens' well-being. i know that all these issues are important also in this parliament and that the european parliament has done a great deal to promote them. (fi) i spoke earlier about how the union can contribute to strengthening security in europe and the world. the security of the people is not just the absence of war, but also economic security, the exercise of human rights and the protection of the rule of law. the tampere european council aims to create a more coherent and solidary immigration and asylum policy and to strengthen the fight against crossborder crime. the member states' legislation in this sector needs to be harmonised and approximated, judgments enforced more effectively and administrative and legal obstacles to cooperation removed. i started with the idea that the union can only succeed if there is close and open cooperation between all the parties involved and if the european citizens actively support it. the european parliament has a crucial role to play in this cooperation. i would like to wish you and the members of this house energy and tenacity, heart and will at the beginning of the session and assure you that the finnish presidency expects a great deal from our cooperation. mr president, between milosevic's thumb and nato's hammer: this is the view of a large part of the serbian democratic opposition to milosevic. between the violence of the serbian paramilitary and paramilitary forces, the intimidation and violence of the kla and the nato bombs falling from the sky: this is the feeling of those albanian forces in kosovo who had chosen to fight for their rights through non-violence; against nato bombing and against milosevic's nationalism and murderous and suicidal policy: this is the feeling of millions and millions of european men and women who believe that respect for human rights is fundamental wherever they are violated: the following are also included in the list: the european union is not a party to the peace process, but a party to the peace process. of course, milosevic was and must be stopped, just as the kla was and must be stopped, each with its own different responsibilities. today, after nato has destroyed and devastated in the name of defending human rights, we must rebuild. we hope and would like to work in this parliament to ensure that reconstruction takes place with respect for and appreciation of the human resources and dignity of each party to the conflict and not with a colonization of europe, of the world bank by the united states. reconstruct kosovo with the protagonism of the people who live and have lived there, the albanians and the serbs; at the same time, rebuild serbia and montenegro, the infrastructure devastated and destroyed by the bombings; repair and investigate environmental damage; the european union is not only a political force, but also a political force. the reconstruction process must therefore involve local communities, with exchanges between municipalities, universities, women and men from the european union, kosovo and the yugoslav republic. reconciliation, building societies, building democracy, so that war can really be out of history: this is our challenge!
mr president, nato intervened in yugoslavia claiming to defend the kosovars from serbian persecution, but no one intervened to defend the kurds from turkish persecution. and this is the focal point of the öcalan issue: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. this is the text of article 125 of the turkish penal code, which, as the turkish ambassador kaleli has said here in strasbourg, is in keeping with article 241 of the italian penal code, which was enacted during the fascist era and is still in force. so as long as the european parliament focuses its attention only on whether or not to execute president öcalan, without addressing the issue of self-determination of peoples in depth, it will scratch the surface of the problem but will never succeed in solving it.
council. - (fi) mr president, time and again, we will return to those issues that have been raised here in these two hours or so. i apologise sincerely for not being able to respond to all the excellent, well-considered and immensely important speeches that we have heard here today. i have solved the time problem to some extent by addressing some of the issues that have been touched on by the majority of members in my response. firstly, the situation in the balkans. in september, when i come to the committee meeting here i shall certainly be once more engaged in the same discussion. however, i would like to answer two questions. firstly, the position on the commission"s agency in thessaloniki, which has been expressed here, comes as no surprise. the commission expressed the same position in the general affairs council. the general affairs council made a unanimous decision, which you are aware of, and which i mentioned in my speech. we begin from the position that the european union will approach the issue of stability in the balkans on a regional basis. accordingly, the headquarters will be in thessaloniki. however, the centre of operations, the first of such, will be in pristina and there will be more of them in other regions as and when facilities improve. i sincerely hope that we, the commission and you yourselves will achieve consensus on this matter. as far as i am aware, we have a tradition that when the heads of our governments and states come to a decision on an issue at a summit, be it a regular or extraordinary meeting, we strive in the general affairs council to find a way of implementing that decision. this is what was done in the case of the thessaloniki office as well as with regard to the broader plan and hombach. as the country holding the presidency, we shall strive to implement, as i said, all those decisions we need in respect of sarajevo and the future. in every respect i agree that we have to avoid excessive bureaucracy. that is not the right way to provide employment for the citizens of the european union. but we do need a sufficient number of skilled officials to take care of the operation. as for the other matters, i would just like to say briefly that despite all the help we shall give the region, ultimately the responsibility obviously lies with the people there themselves. we will only be providing them with support. serbia is a part of the balkans. for this reason, we have looked at great length into ways of helping serbia to achieve democracy as quickly as possible. one condition of the return to democracy is freedom of opinion. in this i agree with what has been said here regarding the safety of those who are speaking for freedom of opinion and also the proposal that we should create, through various means, a structure to support real freedom of opinion for this country. the war is behind us, but the peace has to be built. for this, we shall also need peacekeeping forces. finland is not a member state of nato, though you might perhaps have formed the opinion she was, from some of the speeches we have heard. but finland respects the choice of those eleven eu member states who do belong to nato. finland, which has a population of five million, is making its contribution by sending eight hundred peacekeepers to kosovo. you may compare that figure in terms of the population of your own country. in my opinion, we should all share in the task of ensuring that the balkan region is secure. but as i said before, for this we need a non-military option in addition to the military one. in this connection, i would like to acknowledge the role played by denmark for being the first country to allude clearly to this issue in the resolution passed at the cologne council meeting. denmark was the country to make the proposal, but finland is the country which, as holder of the presidency, will promote this worthy initiative as vigorously as possible in co-operation with others. i would also like to mention that the international war crimes tribunal is, in our opinion, extremely important, and the tribunal dealing with war crimes in yugoslavia is a special partner in co-operation for us. we have been co-operating together in kosovo ever since the report was put together on the mass killings at racak, when the expert opinion of finnish coroners was used. we believe this policy is a coherent one. next, i will briefly mention the tampere summit again. we will strive to strike a balance, a very delicate balance, between human rights and the question of security. the tampere meeting will be preceded by an independent meeting of non-governmental organisations, and we, for our part, wish to offer them our support, while obviously respecting their independent status. the european union must not be allowed to become a fortress: it must be both strong and open at the same time. this is the principle upon which we will operate, with respect to the tampere summit also. the rights of minorities are a weak area in established democracies also, whatever aspect of minorities they are concerned with. i wish to call attention to the fact that there is often room for improvement in all our countries when it comes to the status of ethnic minorities. the roma are a european minority with no host country to speak up for them when their rights are being violated. i have been in touch with the foreign ministry in slovakia, one of the applicant countries, and we have begun talks on how the issue may be solved from their point of view. but there is no cause for complacency: the status of the romain europe is by no means good. in addition, the situation regarding the rights of minorities in turkey has been mentioned here. i spoke of it in my speech, and the kurdish question will most certainly be a subject for debate here very often in the future, when we shall strive to find a way to improve the situation. as for enlargement, we shall accept with seriousness the challenge this presents while finland holds the presidency. as i must respect the order of business i cannot go into this question in any great detail now as it would take up too much time. but i would like to assure you of two things. we shall treat the applicant countries as impartially as possible, on the basis of the commission"s reports, and we shall try to make our decisions wisely so that the applicant countries will feel they have made progress according to their individual merits, believing the process to be fair, efficiently undertaken and conducive to the common good. for this reason, we finns have never made promises in connection with timetables any more than we have regarding other matters. however, in our opinion, enlargement is a special issue, and we will do everything within our power in the area of institutional reform to move it along. institutional reforms must be introduced in such a way that the union can live with them even when the number of member states has exceeded twenty. finally, i have to commend the many here who have spoken of a socially and ecologically strong europe. that gives me hope. i mentioned it today to a group representing young people in europe, who met me, as a representative of the european union, at a writing competition award ceremony. i assured them that we would be giving consideration to the issue of youth, although there is no longer any special portfolio attached to this issue. issues of youth can be successfully dealt with by means of mainstreaming, just as effectively as, for example, issues of gender equality, aged people, as has been touched upon here, and other such matters. i believe that we have to discuss the matter again with the new commission, and also with you here in the european parliament. i have tried to be as brief as possible to keep to the schedule, and in keeping with the spirit of the new, efficient european union.
the debate is closed. the vote will take place on thursday at 12.00 p.m.

council . – (fi) mr president, we have been unanimous in this debate. the actions of mr bangemann, a member of the commission, in this affair can in no way be approved. i would like to thank those who have spoken for having given their approval of the council"s decision to take swift action in this affair, and for their support in bringing the case to court. i would like to underline the importance of mr prodi"s code of practice in this connection, and i wish to refer to mr elles" speech in which he said that hopefully we will not be saying ‘too little, too late’ next time. we must take care this time, then, that codes of conduct are absolutely clear and legally binding on all parties. in my view, it is an issue that concerns us all as it is sometimes hard for the public, as was mentioned here, to distinguish between the institutions of the union, which means that events such as this should not be allowed to occur in any of the institutions. thank you.
commission. – i should like to begin by saying that mrs read was absolutely right to draw attention to the moderate way in which the resolution is couched and indeed the moderation of the language throughout the whole of this debate. it would have been easy for people to try to make a populist appeal. that was almost entirely avoided. that simply added to the weight of the opinion of this house and therefore to the reaction which everyone has just heard from mrs sasi, president-in-office of the council. mr elles was quite right to emphasise that it is absolutely essential to see responsibility exercised to the highest standard. i hope – to take mr barón crespo's point – that this experience is not repeated, that we can ensure that specific and very clear provision is made so that there cannot be any repetition of the experience of recent weeks. there are those who would say, and they have, i believe, rationality on their side, that the provisions of article 213 of the treaty and its references to integrity and discretion mean that for anyone who has an understanding of required standards in public life, little more should need to be said, if anything. but if it is necessary – and i am conscious of being subject to the fact this issue is now before the court – even then to be more specific about what the implications of integrity and discretion are, then i am glad the house welcomes the developments that will take place as a consequence of the initiative taken by the president designate. i look forward to ensuring that there is a full response to both the appeal of mr barón crespo and that of mr elles to the demonstration of the highest standards of public integrity. i would also say that mr kreissl-dörfler was right to emphasise, together with mr duff – in a very interesting maiden speech – that if all institutions do not demonstrate the highest standards of rectitude for themselves, by themselves, then those standards will be imposed externally, either in the form of punishment by the general public, initially through its apathy and detachment but maybe in other ways more directly, politically too, or by the council taking a responsibility which others appear to be unwilling to demonstrate. i do not believe the european union, its institutions or its people would be enriched by that experience. it is a fair warning to all of us who serve the institutions and the european people that we are responsible for our own destiny and our own standards and we must therefore proceed without any form of delay to ensuring that those standards are as stringent as possible. i was interested in the point made in another fine maiden speech by miss van der laan which calls for a response. i am happy to report to the house that she is a friend of some years and therefore my joy in witnessing her maiden speech was very particular. i see it was shared in other parts of the house. she said that it would be ‘better if the commission had taken the step of reference to the court of justice’. i should like to say to her, therefore, that the commission, as i reported earlier, discussed the matter in its meeting of 1 july. in that meeting the feeling was expressed that the issues relating to mr bangemann and to article 213 had legal complexities that could not be speedily resolved. for that reason the commission made plain its view in its statement of 1 july that there needed to be clarification of implementation of article 213. there was no consensus at that meeting which supported reference to the court of justice at that stage. i say that to ensure that members of this house understand that the issue is live, it has been a matter of discussion, the action has now been taken by the council and that determines the path for the future. but there is no lack of concern or consideration in the current commission, despite the fact that it is coming to the end of its life, in these issues. i conclude by saying that mrs laguiller made an extremely valuable contribution to this debate because, like others, she dramatised the issue quite properly by drawing attention to the economic gaps in society and the way in which those divisions actually serve to emphasise the fact that it is absolutely essential for those of us who have the good fortune to hold representative and appointed positions in the public service to act with complete integrity and discretion. we enjoy a very considerable privilege. we must, therefore, show complete probity. this debate has served to underlined that. if anybody ever needed the lesson, this has added to it. i hope we can look forward to a future where no instruction will be necessary.

madam president, with regard to the joint resolution adopted on kosovo, and particularly paragraph 11, which seems to be a moment for dialogue between the different positions, i think it is important, in the context of the explanation of vote, to highlight certain aspects. one of these is linked to the structure of the agency for kosovo and its reconstruction. i think that the identification of a decentralised and flexible structure to improve its effectiveness is a very important element, but one which must be linked to a more comprehensive assessment. since 1990, certain areas of europe, of southern italy, and particularly puglia, have been experiencing a discomfort linked to their geographical location in relation to migratory flows. the conflict in kosovo has further complicated this. in assessing the situation, i think it is crucial to have a more comprehensive assessment of the problem and to try to identify solutions which, in the context of the elasticity of the structure, can highlight certain aspects related to the problems of these territories in southern italy.

the denial, once again, of the rights of minorities in this parliament. so we can gargle on minorities all over the world, we can keep going in that direction. but perhaps we should start to realise that the european construction is based on such undemocratic foundations that many of the countries of the world, which we often criticize, have nothing to envy to the so-called democratic system we have today. so perhaps we should go back to the essentials, refuse the council's offer of a dog's bone and start asking for fundamental reforms, including the reform we must ask ourselves, so that this parliament becomes a real parliament and not the instrument of bureaucracies, as it is becoming more and more.
- (it) madam president, the motion for a resolution contains, in my view, all the assessments and proposals which parliament should approve in order to make a timely contribution to the tampere council's implementation of the solemn promises made to the european citizens in the treaty of amsterdam. the aim is simple: to achieve together what each member state would do less well on its own. no one is under any illusion that the member states alone can effectively combat organized crime, whose frauds cross borders and which is involved in the cruel trafficking of children, women and refugees. we must work hand in hand, but any extension of the union's powers in the field of crime prevention and repression must be done in a context where each person has the guarantee that his own rights will also be respected. there is no freedom without security, but there is no security without justice. justice means first of all respect for the principle of equality, the principle of non-discrimination, respect for the equal dignity of each person, respect for diversity. the union's policy must be based on this principle. the proposed document is based on this principle. we know that the member states are resistant - although not all of them declare it - to this matter which traditionally falls within national competence. but the resistance, the non-execution and the delay have only the effect of causing harm, and we have no time for referrals. the enlargement of the union to include the new applicant countries requires, on the contrary, the immediate opening of negotiations with a view to the gradual adoption of common measures and adequate guarantee standards. i hope that the finnish presidency, whose statements i appreciated this morning, will seek to take credit for having started to fulfil the promises contained in the treaty of amsterdam.
- (it) madam president, the decision of the cologne council to draw up a charter of fundamental rights will involve our parliament in a long and complex work. the nature of this charter, its content, the relationship between the charter of rights and the constitutionalisation process of the union will have to be discussed. these topics will be the subject of a report which the committee on constitutional affairs will prepare, in collaboration with other committees, in the coming months and which it will present to you all in plenary. at the moment, however, it is urgent to take a position on the procedure to be followed for the drawing up of this charter. that is the limited but important objective of the resolution which mr watson and i have tabled. parliament must say today, before the council decides on the constitution of the body responsible for drawing up the draft charter, that it cannot accept that the number of representatives in the european parliament be less than that of the representatives of the heads of state or government of the member states. it should also request careful consultation before deciding whether the essential contribution of national parliaments should be ensured through the participation of a number of their representatives in the envisaged body or through other more effective channels. i now make a personal comment on the fight against crime and the challenge of immigration. my experience as minister of the interior in italy tells me that it is essential to speed up the creation of common regulations and policies, the exact opposite of invoking purely national policies. and as far as immigration and asylum are concerned, the best basis is the document produced by the austrian presidency in autumn 1998.
counsel. - (fi) madam president, unfortunately i cannot answer all the questions in the time available, but we have noted them and, as the discussions and preparations continue, i will try, by all available means, to take account of the ideas contained therein and to provide you with more answers. the question raised by many speakers concerning the preparation of the charter of fundamental rights is of course indirectly related to the tampere meeting, since we are preparing to take a further step forward in ensuring that the people of the european union have a safer space than before, but the preparation of the fundamental rights dossier is not the actual subject of the tampere discussions. a special body will be set up for this purpose, in which the european parliament will be represented. as regards the representation of the european parliament in tampere, you are no doubt aware that mrs fontaine will be present at the beginning of the meeting, that we are also aware of your resolution and that the discussions here will also be taken into account. i have selected a few of the questions that were raised during this session and will now answer them. firstly, mr pirker's question on the immigration strategy: it is an important basis for the discussions in tampere, but there is still no unanimity in the council on this. that is why we will of course be discussing it in tampere, but no document is ready yet. as regards the eurodac regulation, we are trying to get it approved now, during our presidency, but as you will no doubt know, we are awaiting a new opinion from the european parliament on this subject. as regards mr watson's question on respect for the right of asylum and cooperation with the unhcr, we are in full agreement. it is on this basis that i have built my speech. i fully agree with you that europe must not close itself off from the outside world, but must be open to it. we are present at the un, the council of europe, the osce and this is how we can create natural relations with the outside world. as regards mr wuori's question, i would like to say briefly that finland respects transparency, and although mr wuori has never been a member of the finnish parliament, he is certainly aware that in finland, as in other member states, the national parliament has a certain degree of autonomy, so that a member of the government cannot intervene in such a matter. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's citizens are not exposed to the dangers of the internet. finland has considered the current control to be acceptable as long as we have not made further progress, but afterwards the control system will have to be developed. i also note his remark about electronic systems and i promise to look into it. to the question of mr schulz and other speakers on the legal area and fundamental rights, i would like to reply that i have stressed that the area of freedom, security and justice must be built on the basis of common values, respect for human rights and greater integration. i would like to mention one more thing. i am very pleased that many speakers have said that we must not make the european union a fortress, but rather a safe area that will have a natural relationship with its environment. i would like to remind you once again that the european union is not the same as europe and that the world outside the european union is not only poverty, hunger and despair. there are problems, of course, but there are also countries with a very high level of civilization and who are willing to cooperate with us. i would like to give as examples norway, iceland and switzerland. i would also like to remind you that our task is to work in the united nations as well as in the osce and the council of europe. the principle of democracy is that humans are not just objects of power, they are also subjects of power, and we must remember this when building cooperative relationships with them. within the european union, we really do not always need new directives. often it is simply a matter of complying with decisions already made. it is also a question of strengthening and implementing in practice the valid un or council of europe agreements and of inducing other member countries to do the same. i may have listened with a little more sensitive ear than usual to your complaints about the lack of cooperation following the plane crash in greece, but i would like to assure you on behalf of the council that in the european union we must have confidence in each other that national democracy is here, in your midst, complementing the european union's own democracy. i am sure that they are the right combination, as many speakers have said, to enable us in the future to draw up the charter of fundamental rights and to take the tampere meeting and its principles further, and not just to make declarations, so that we can also make progress in practical matters. i hope i can also gain your trust in this case.
counsel. - (fi) madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the european union has from the outset supported the efforts of portugal and indonesia to achieve a lasting solution for east timor, under the auspices of the united nations, which would be accepted by the international community and based on the right of self-determination of the east timorese people. the union is continuing its determined action to achieve this objective. in the general affairs council decision of april 1999, we asked the commission to prepare economic and social programmes to improve the availability of facilities for the establishment of institutions in east timor. the east timorese's enthusiasm for the elections is worthy of respect, as we all know. the general affairs council said on monday that the east timorese deserve our full support in the future as well. we are ready to recognise east timor as soon as the un-led independence process has been successfully completed. i would like to thank unamet (the united nations mission in east timor) for the tireless work of its staff in carrying out the referendum in very difficult circumstances. at the same time i would like to say that i am grateful to mr andrews, the irish foreign minister, who was the president's personal representative in observing the vote in east timor, and to mr cook, the british foreign minister, who represented the president at the apec meeting. we wanted the council of ministers to be represented at these events, although the president was unable to attend because of his obligations arising from the plenary session of parliament. i would also like to thank the delegation of european parliament observers and all those who took part in the observation of the vote: the international presence was very important in the success of the vote. i believe they helped create a climate of security which was still present during the vote. we were all afraid of trouble. the terror that began after the vote, however, was surprisingly violent. throughout the crisis, the union has exerted strong pressure on the indonesian government to fulfil its obligations to ensure security and to keep the situation in east timor under control. i think we're on the right track, but we still have a long way to go. relieving human suffering and mobilizing international peacekeeping forces are the most urgent things and they now have priority. the sooner the peacekeepers get there, the better. that is why the european union is pursuing its own policy of pressure to make this happen. the second very urgent thing is the rapid delivery of humanitarian aid. the most difficult situation is currently faced by the refugees who have fled to the mountains, as well as by east timorese who have been moved to west timor and, in part, settled in camps. some estimates put the number of refugees at between 200,000 and 400,000. it also shows that estimates of the situation are very vague. we do not have accurate information about the events because the outside representatives of the international community do not have access to the area. these people are in need of immediate humanitarian assistance. intergovernmental organisations, as well as non-governmental organisations, participate in un-coordinated relief activities. the indonesian authorities announced that the doors were open to international aid, but that they could not guarantee the safety of humanitarian personnel. a large-scale aid operation can only be undertaken when humanitarian organisations have free and safe access to the region. this can only be done with the help of international peacekeeping forces, but we have already demanded again that humanitarian aid be able to reach the area immediately. several countries are prepared to participate in the delivery of humanitarian aid. stocks are being counted and deliveries are being arranged. the eu has eight million euro for humanitarian aid in east timor, making it the largest single donor. representatives of echo (european community's humanitarian organisation) participated in the assessment of aid needs with the indonesian authorities and international organisations. i hope that the commission will be able to give us more precise information on its plans. humanitarian aid will probably be airlifted and basic necessities such as food, medicine, blankets and cooking supplies will be parachuted into areas where refugees are known to be. experts say that for safety reasons, parachuting will initially be done by hercules transport planes, which will be one and a half kilometers high, making it difficult to reach the destination safely. however, every effort must be made to ensure that the aid is sufficient in this phase. security will only really improve when international troops arrive. the union has always stressed and demanded that indonesia be responsible for law and order and security, but as we have seen, the indonesian authorities have unfortunately not taken care of their contractual obligations in this regard. indonesia has now agreed to the arrival of international peacekeeping forces in the region and i have just learned that the un security council has unanimously decided to send peacekeepers to east timor. we assume that indonesia will continue to cooperate well with the security council. we are working to get the troops on the ground as quickly as possible. i would like to add that i have personally tried to contact the member states of the union, in particular portugal, our representation in indonesia and the united nations secretary-general, mr kofi annan, by telephone, and i hope that in the future the possibilities for closer cooperation will be improved. i would also like to mention the seriousness of the acts of terrorism and the climate of fear in east timor. indonesia has failed to live up to its commitments to ensure order and security in the region, and many believe that it has no desire to do so either. arbitrariness has prevailed in the region. we have evidence that the indonesian military and police have been involved in acts of terror on the island. according to humanitarian workers from the international community, representatives of the icrc (international committee of the red cross) and human rights organizations were forced to leave the scene so that the abuses could be carried out without the presence of outside witnesses. the council expressed its anger on this matter the day before yesterday, as well as on the attacks directed against east timorese, humanitarian workers, church representatives and human rights defenders. we in the council supported the proposal of the committee on human rights to convene an extraordinary session on east timor. we will also take steps to ensure that a commission of inquiry is sent to the region as soon as possible to gather evidence of the acts of terrorism committed after the referendum, in order to establish responsibility. the general affairs council accepted the conclusions on the activities of the union the day before yesterday and decided on a common position reinforcing them, providing for a very broad embargo on exports to indonesia of arms, ammunition and other instruments of internal oppression and terror. bilateral military cooperation between the member states and indonesia was also suspended. this common position will be in force for four months and will be renewed if necessary. while we are working for the realization of the right of self-determination of east timor and its people, we must also be concerned about the future development of indonesia. the general affairs council stressed the union's desire to see a strong, democratic and united indonesia. democracy, respect for human rights and the rule of law are the cornerstones on which indonesia can build. we shall support any attitude which is consistent with a democratic society in an international community.

counsel. -(fi) mr president, the fight for the abolition of the death penalty is an essential element of the european union's general human rights policy. in june 1998 the council adopted guidelines for community policy towards third countries on issues related to the death penalty. the ultimate objective of the european union is to achieve the abolition of the death penalty in all countries of the world. to this end, the council urges all countries still applying the death penalty to establish a moratorium on the execution of these sentences and requires that minimum standards be observed. it also calls on states to adhere to international legal instruments prohibiting the death penalty. in line with the guidelines it has adopted, the european union took the initiative by presenting for the first time a draft resolution on the death penalty at the 55th session of the un commission on human rights in geneva this year. this initiative was particularly successful, the content was more direct than that of previous resolutions and, moreover, it received more supporters - 72 - than in 1998, when there were 65. on the occasion of the meeting of the commission on human rights, the european union also organised a debate meeting on the death penalty, which was attended by ngos and government representatives from different countries. the council has just decided to continue its policy of raising the issue of the death penalty in multilateral fora and has decided to present for the first time a proposal for a resolution on the death penalty to the 54th session of the united nations general assembly. the council is convinced that this initiative will further strengthen the international movement towards the abolition of the death penalty.
- mr president, first of all, i would like to ask you to look a little to the left and right of the house because i had indicated first that i wished to ask a supplementary question earlier and as you did not see me, i was unable to do so. i thank the finnish presidency for its work at the united nations on the abolition of the death penalty, and i am very pleased to know that, thanks to the finnish presidency - and not only, of course - we will have a resolution in new york this year. the whole problem, i think, is the question of the text of the assembly. i have been informed that this text is modelled on the text adopted in geneva. the only major defect is that it does not provide for successive deadlines by which the initiative to institute the moratorium could be implemented by means of a treaty or other institutional initiative. could the council presidency give me further information on this? what kind of possible timetable does the council have in mind for this initiative to be put into practice?
counsel. -{}}} mr president, like the honourable member, the council is gravely concerned about the human rights violations that have occurred in the democratic republic of korea, particularly those of political prisoners, and also about the failure to respect the principles of the rule of law and the refusal of the authorities to cooperate with international human rights organisations. the european union also considers that the north korean difficulties are mainly structural and that it is itself responsible for them. in view of the evolution of the regime in the dprk and the path largely chosen by the dprk itself, the european union is in favour of giving north korea the opportunity to respond positively to this commitment. the european union conducted its first political dialogue with the democratic republic of korea in december 1998. it is prepared to hold a second similar meeting later this year, depending on the progress made by the dprk in the various areas where the european union considers the situation to be of concern. on this point, the european union urged the dprk to fully respect the commitments of the nuclear non-proliferation treaty and to sign and ratify a nuclear test ban treaty. the democratic republic of korea should stop its missile flight tests, which are undermining the stability of the region. the european union urges the dprk to cease exports of missiles and missile technology to unstable and sensitive regions of the world. the european union is also very concerned about possible new missile tests. it invites third countries in its political contacts to try to influence the dprk to respect all these commitments. on 19 july, the council of the european union adopted important resolutions on a commission communication on the republic of korea. the resolutions also address the situation on the korean peninsula and the european union's concerns, which i have mentioned before.

the rapporteur. - (it) mr president, the era of peace and prosperity to which arafat and other israeli leaders have referred since 1993, after the oslo agreements, has not yet arrived. the resumption of the peace process after the netanyahu government, the election of barak and the new agreement signed in sharm el-sheikh are undoubtedly a step forward from the mists of the past after the assassination of prime minister rabin, but the road is still difficult before a stable and lasting peace is achieved and before the palestinians can live, as sanctioned by un resolutions, in a palestinian state which i hope will be democratic and will be able to coexist peacefully with the state of israel. the problems are still numerous: 70% of the west bank-gaza territory is still occupied by the military; the status of jerusalem is not yet defined; the jewish settlements, the confiscation of land and water; the number of political prisoners, which still stands at over 2 200; the question of the palestinian refugees of the first exodus of 1948 and 1967, who number about four million. without a positive response to these problems, peace will always be in danger. the european union and in particular the european parliament have always expressed their unconditional support for the efforts to promote the middle east peace process and to provide assistance to the region. in its march 1999 resolution on the peace process and future assistance in the middle east, the european parliament rightly stressed that even if the wye plantation agreements were confirmed - and they were - the most important problems still had to be resolved, among them in particular the status of refugees. for this reason, the commission has recognised the need for continued and rigorous support for refugees and has approved the tenth ec-unrwa convention covering the years 1999-2001 - which has been submitted to the european parliament and represents an important contribution to this end. the proposed convention, like the previous nine signed after 1972, sets the ec contribution to the budget of the agency's regular programmes for the next three years (1999-2001) and also provides for annual negotiations on the european contribution to the food aid budget. of course, the content of food aid will be the subject of separate negotiations. unrwa is requesting a total contribution of eur 120.82 million for the period up to 2001. part of this contribution is intended to support unrwa's education programmes. in the health programme, the bulk of it will be allocated to the education programme. the regular programme of unrwa is aimed at some 3 500 000 palestinians living in jordan, syria, lebanon, jordan, the west bank and the gaza strip and, as i said earlier, provides assistance in vital social sectors. in order to take account of the changing political situation in the region, article 6 of the convention stipulates that, by the end of 2000, the parties will review the situation of refugees and evaluate possible plans by unrwa to transfer its functions to the palestinian authority. since the palestinian authority controls only the west bank and gaza, special attention must be paid to the work of unrwa in support of the refugees still living in syria, lebanon and jordan and in all the countries where they are counted. in these circumstances, i would like to stress once again that the refugee problem has not only not been solved, but is getting worse by the day, as i have seen during my recent visits to refugee camps in lebanon, jordan and syria and in the various reports of unrwa and mr menendez del valle as the european union's representative for humanitarian aid in jordan. it is therefore essential that this ec-unrwa convention be adopted and implemented as soon as possible. this is particularly important in view of the current crisis affecting unrwa's budget. austerity measures were taken by cutting off several funds for teachers, hospitals and medical supplies, which caused a strong protest in the palestinian refugee camps. a final question: while stressing the need to continue to provide humanitarian aid through unrwa, i believe that the european parliament has a particular interest in finding a just and equitable political solution to the problem of the palestinian refugees, a solution which is in line with un resolution 194. in this connection, i believe that maximum efforts must be made to invite the relevant forces in the region, in particular the israeli and palestinian authorities, to contribute to this solution. the committee on budgets has proposed two amendments in favour of greater transparency, which we support. finally, there is the legal problem of how the commission is asking for our vote, which i think we will have to study.

- (it) mr prodi, you were right to say that scientific information is always incomplete. it is still incomplete in the sense that it is improving every day, but it is the duty of politicians to realise that we should not experiment on citizens until we have certainty in certain areas. you mentioned a white paper on food safety. we are interested in your choice and we all hope that you will not propose a white paper like that of mr delors: a white paper on unemployment which, as far as i am concerned, has not created a single job. we hope that this white paper will contribute to the health of citizens. we want rules to be established without further delay. we want to know who controls and who controls the controllers, as an old latin saying goes. the delays since march - the crisis having only broken out in may following the announcement of the dioxin crisis in belgium - reflect inadequate food safety controls and demonstrate that the analysis of the daily diet for the possible presence of pcbs and dioxins or other pollutants is practically non-existent or only partially carried out. let's move on to another problem. it seems that the retail industry often likes to put thawed meat back into toxic substances so that it can be resold to the public. in fact, several past inquiries have shown this. in 1990, and the scandal broke in the press in 1992, we denounced the case of the famous blood gelatin steaks - a product from england, again - which hit consumers hard. we know all the components of dioxin: chlorine, carbon, hydrogen, oxygen. it's insoluble in water, it resists temperatures, it stays present for hundreds of years. i don't think it's wise to continue to ingest it every day. the animal feed contains meat meal and therefore slaughter residues with mixtures of blood, feathers and carcasses. since 1995, following the mad cow case, the european union, the commission, has decreed that it is now forbidden to feed meat meal to ruminants. meat flour was good for fish, chicken, sheep, and pigs. the result: the continuation of this system has led to the situation in belgium. the chickens continued to eat meat flour. how did we discover this " super-pollution " ? when the threshold of 1,500 extra points was passed, the eggs would not even open to release the chicks. so we thought, maybe something's wrong. to hell with the controls ! so, since 1995, meat meal has been replaced by fish meal. but if we do some research - and there have been few such studies in recent years - we find that fish meal contains a high percentage of meat meal, which is made up of carcasses and butcher's feathers. it seems to me that only today has france had the intelligence to say: stop, wait a minute, let us switch to plant-based animal feed because we can no longer guarantee the safety of our consumers. and now there have been rumours since august that the european union wants to allow the inclusion of meat meal up to 0.15% in finished animal feed. but let us realize, let us calculate carefully: 0.15% of 4 000 tonnes of feed for cattle is equivalent to 6 tonnes: if we are to try to survive, this percentage seems unacceptable to me! the mario negri institute in milan has shown that the most serious source of pcb contamination among italians comes from the consumption of dairy products, meat and fish. we should also bring up the problem of taxation in this debate. - (it) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, dioxin and bse are two different matters which have hit the world of agricultural production hard and have directly affected the consumer. these two problems have been presented to the public as two exceptional cases, but given the frequency of similar cases within the union, we believe that ordinary solutions are needed, not more "buffers". dioxin is the latest and most striking evidence of this. we must look for stable and lasting solutions. the solutions must be based on three very specific aspects: 1) the consumer; 2) a new agro-environmental revolution; 3) a precise political orientation which must come precisely from the community bodies. in this context, producers and consumers give a largely positive rating: on the one hand, agricultural entrepreneurs - now long and strongly bound to strict production rules - fully guarantee and support the maturity of the consumer, as history and current events demonstrate. on the other hand, the consumer himself represents a possible solution to the problem, a consumer who has a good understanding of the european policy on brands and typical products, giving weight to local european products, which are perfectly capable of withstanding international competition which, on the other hand, favours mass-produced food. in this context, we cannot ask producers to sacrifice themselves in a problem which too often causes them to succumb and makes them losers in the market for faults for which they are not responsible. they must then regain hard-won confidence, with production costs here too often underestimated or at least only partially assessed. it takes a lot of time. the question of production costs is not mentioned anywhere: neither the rural development regulation nor the consumer policy regulation make any reference to this aspect. we must learn from cases like dioxin and realise that if farmers are entitled to higher incomes in the case of higher environmental standards, such conditions should also be applied at the level of food, all the more so when it comes to food production. so if the alienation of products is demonstrated, in cases similar to the dioxin scandal, then it is appropriate to consider a special area for the enhancement of quality. the concept of quality has been - and still is - the subject of frequent discussion. i myself will take up here a precise but complex tangle of characteristics: hygiene, traceability, safety, precise production characteristics - also environmental - nutritional values, etc. but quality - not the quality of bulk products which i mentioned in reference to the world market - must be recognized both in normal situations and, for even stronger reasons, in conditions of almost constant environmental risks for the producer. agenda 2000 and regulation 1257 recognise and emphasise aspects of a model which is moving in this direction. - (it) madam president, i should like to begin by congratulating president prodi on the scientific rigor but also on the great transparency of his speech. i clearly agree with the preamble to commission decision 640 of 23 september 1999 which, on the basis of the who, emphasizes that dioxin is one of the most carcinogenic substances in existence, which is why the who has recommended that the acceptable daily intake should be maintained at 1-4 picograms per kilogram of body weight. however, i cannot help but be puzzled and concerned - both as a scientist and as a politician - by the conclusions we have reached in this decision, namely the establishment of a maximum tolerance level for pcbs of 200 nanograms per gram of fat, whether for poultry, meat or pork products. i wonder, mr president, who can guarantee that the dioxin levels present are not dangerous to man, even with this reference to pcbs? in the end, the hazardousness is not only related to the level, but also depends on the typical pharmacokinetic characteristics of these compounds. these have a half-life of about 5.8 years, or even 7 years, which means that they remain present for about ten years after being absorbed. so, continuous absorption certainly creates an accumulation that can be dangerous. i therefore think that the directives of the commission, which you are chairing, should focus on the permanent monitoring of the levels of dioxins, but also of other pollutants, in all the food we eat, as well as in the air we breathe and the water we drink. in this context, i believe that we need clear guidelines, in the interests of citizens and consumers, so that human exposure levels to dioxin and pcbs - which belong to the same class as dioxin - are as low as possible. in conclusion, i share mr florenz's view of this european agency. it is extremely useful, but it is of little use, mr president, if it is not installed on the ground, as close as possible to the citizens, through what i suggested to the finnish minister for health. highly qualified laboratories are therefore needed in every region of europe, laboratories close to the citizen, providing scientific assurance on what we absorb or breathe every day. all this can be done, and i've already made it clear, this can be done with the help of the qualified laboratories that already exist in universities. problem we are facing calls, in my view, for a response that goes beyond the political ideas that often divide us. i can assure you that the commission is keen to strengthen the measures taken in this
