seriousness of the problem for the younger generation. i have also raised this issue and other speakers have also stressed it. if we want to help give hope to africa, we have to do something here. the figures quoted by several speakers speak for themselves. i think the situation is very serious and i share the view expressed in the debate that this is more than a health problem. mrs maij-weggen stressed that these societies were in fact collapsing and that the progress made was being undermined. i think the numbers are of enormous importance. if the rest of the world had better statistics and more information on the real gravity of the situation, our people would be more aware of the need to increase our efforts in africa, including on a more general level, because the current problem is linked to the general situation in africa. statistics also play a vital role in helping those who wish to combat taboos on the problem. it would be very useful if zimbabwe could give us accurate official statistics - not only on the dangers and the number of people affected in zimbabwe, but also on the results that have been achieved in the neighbouring countries, tanzania, uganda and senegal. the more the authorities are open to the problem, the more positive the results will be. the discussion on condom use also showed a broad consensus on the importance of making condoms accessible and inexpensive and of giving people accurate and honest information, as was the case in tanzania, uganda and senegal. in this connection, i would like to share with you an experience i had in the district of binga, zimbabwe. there, 25 percent of women giving birth for the first time are hiv-positive, and over 30 percent of women giving birth in the local hospital are also hiv-positive. this is a terrible situation. we visited some nuns who were involved in an aids project. i asked them if the question of condom use was part of their information work. they said they could only accept and recommend the use of condoms when it came to prostitutes or related groups. they wanted moral force to be used as a means of defense. it was a very tough attitude and i feel that when we know the damage that results, we have an obligation to share our knowledge with people who need it; i would tend to say that we must impose this knowledge on them. i think that this duty and this moral aspect are issues which could take the discussion forward or that we could also place this discussion in a context of conditionality which we do not hesitate to use with regard to these very poor countries, which need our partnership and our help. and it can be added that in terms of conditionality, when we talk about structural adjustment and macroeconomic aid, it would be useful to address these conditionality issues in relation to the ability of these countries to guarantee the survival of their own peoples. we must of course avoid taking a too rigid position on this issue, but there is at least a moral aspect to it which makes it wrong for us to accept that certain countries will turn a blind eye. survival is a human right. and when we talk about the modern and expanded concept of human rights, we must include the newly infected babies in zimbabwe, who are our partners in human rights, not the circles that deliberately turn a blind eye and refuse to share with the population their knowledge of treatment and especially prevention. the budgetary problem was raised by almost all the participants in the debate. i would like to say that the problem of discovering, from the various budget lines, the actions of the commission as a whole in a particular field is a variant of a very general problem. you can say that again. we are in the middle of an operation which involves many other budget lines than the one which was discussed during the discussion. it is an operation designed to simplify and clarify the budget. that's what the discussion is about. we cannot find out what is being done about hiv or aids by simply looking at the budget line we have been talking about. that being said, i am the first to admit that our budget does not really reveal - and it is hardly ever found in our reports - what we are doing in this area. the way in which the reports are drawn up and the information work carried out shows a great weakness. it's a warning shot to tell us there's a problem. we must be able to report accurately to our own public opinion on the results achieved in relation to the money spent. i would therefore say that it is not so important to know which budget line is concerned. as i said in the introduction, we have set aside eur 25 million for additional actions in this area and this money comes from different sources. it is therefore clear that it is not just this specific budget line that is a sign of our commitment. mr wijkman spoke of relations with other organisations and another speaker quoted the who. the eu is beginning to open up to other actors with whom we can collaborate and coordinate our efforts - other actors than the 15 member states. this is an important factor in the process of quality improvement, especially for the beneficiaries. i would therefore like to say that this is a goal which we will try to achieve. these are the comments i wanted to make. i thank you again for holding this debate. i would like to conclude by saying that i would very much like to see some of the issues raised today - if the opportunity arises on the basis of the questions to which i shall have to answer - discussed when the acp-eu joint assembly meets in nassau in a week's time and to be able to convey the same messages as those which have marked this debate. - thank you, commissioner nielson. - thank you very much. i have received six motions for resolutions pursuant to rule 37 (2) of the rules of procedure. see minutes of the sitting. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow.
- the following reports by mr martin, on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the proposal for a council decision on the granting of additional macrofinancial assistance to bulgaria (com (99) 0403 - c5-0098/1999 - 1999/0165 (cns)) are requested for joint debate: - a5-0018/1999 by mr martin, on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the proposal for a council decision on the granting of additional macrofinancial assistance to the former yugoslav republic of macedonia (com (99) 0404 - c5-0099/1999 - 1999/0166 (cns)) - a5-0019/1999 by mr martin, on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the proposal for a council decision on the granting of additional macrofinancial assistance to romania (com (99) 0405 - c5-0097/1999 - 1999/0167 (cns)).
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would also like to stress once again, in my first speech to this house, something i have already stressed to the commission, namely the need to establish the best possible relationship between my responsibilities in the commission and those of this house in relation to the relevant matters. i think i have noticed in the course of the various speeches that no group will, in principle, raise any difficulties with aid, but i can see a clear position in favour of the proposals. we all agree that the very difficult kosovo crisis is leading us to make greater efforts. but i would like to make a few points which i think can help us to understand the commission's position on the various amendments tabled. firstly, it is a balance of payments aid that we are talking about. balance of payments support is very different from funding for normal programmes. balance of payments aid is aid to consolidate a country's reserves so that it can cope with its external payment problems, which always have a counterpart: one always has to pay either for the supply of a service or for the purchase of a commodity. we are not talking about funds for the budgets of each of these countries, which are intended to carry out concrete actions. the commission has also proposed that the community should be able to provide the community with a financial contribution to the development of the community's energy sector. this is a financial contribution to meet these difficulties. secondly, in the case of bulgaria and romania, the funds are linked to agreements with the international monetary fund which pursue the same objective. in the case of the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, the situation is somewhat different. it is negotiating an agreement with the international monetary fund. if the negotiations are successful, we will participate in the agreements, but if they are not successful, we believe that the situation is such that the european union's support will be essential anyway. therefore, whether or not there is an agreement with the international monetary fund, there will be support for the former yugoslav republic of macedonia, but of course with the general condition that the reform programme necessary for these funds to be effective is implemented. i would remind you that this is not the first time that aid of this kind has been granted to these countries. aid has been given in the past and has always proved effective. we have never had any difficulties in repaying debts, there has never been a moratorium, and we therefore believe that they are absolutely reliable members in financial terms. that said, i would like to address the various proposals that mr martin has put forward in his report. i think that, according to the philosophy i have explained, our position is understandable. as regards mr martin's first proposal, i am in full agreement with what is being proposed, if i understand correctly. finally, growth and economic reforms will lead to better employment and a better standard of living. we are in complete agreement on both of these points. we would therefore accept a formula which says, instead of your proposal, with a view to increasing employment and living standards. this formula would be entirely acceptable to the commission. we are now moving to the second point, the second amendment, which seeks to introduce budgetary control by the commission in respect of the reference funds. given the different nature of these funds compared to normal projects for which funds are entered in the budget, i fear that in this case the idea of budgetary control, which we all accept and defend, is not exactly applicable. here, control must be carried out differently. what needs to be checked is whether, overall, the country is using the funds correctly. we therefore agree with what we consider to be a fundamental idea, namely that the commission should ensure that the reforms which need to be carried out in these countries are carried out in order to be able to use aid properly. however, we do not think that this is a point to be included in the text of the decision. we will commit ourselves to the commission exercising this control and to the fact that this control is carried out, of course in accordance with the evolution of the macroeconomic powers. we think this type of control will be sufficient. but, as i said, we do not think that the proposed amendment is the best way of putting it. as regards amendment no 3, the main idea is, as has been said, to ensure that part of these resources is devoted to the basic social needs of the population. we are faced here with the problem of the nature of aid, to which i have already referred. if it were a question of budgetary aid, we could quite accept the proposal you are making. but this is financial support for reserves to make a certain type of counterpart. it is not a question of whether or not to support the basic needs of the population. however, to reassure you, i will say that the cases we are talking about are extremely different from the indonesian case you mentioned. as far as the actual assistance from the international monetary fund is concerned, the fund's overall plan for the way in which the budgets and expenditure of these countries are to be defined clearly states that a certain percentage of expenditure will have to be allocated to social expenditure. romania, to give a concrete example - i have the figures here - is currently spending 8.3% of its gdp on social spending for 1997. but the condition set by the fund in its programme is that social spending should rise to 10.5% of gdp by 1999. so these matters of the international monetary fund should not concern you at all. i do not think, however, that it is the commission's job to define in the national budgets how the overall budget is to be drawn up, since, as i have said, these funds have no specific purpose of any kind. of course, we could say the same about amendment no 4, which has a similar justification and tackles problems of the same nature. i would like to add one point to amendment no 3. there are specific union programmes devoted to improving administration and meeting basic social needs. remember that, for example, in the phare programme, 30% of the resources are allocated to institution building, of course linked to anything that improves the administrative functioning that will solve the problems. for these reasons, amendments nos 3 and 4 are not acceptable to us. on the other hand, we are in full agreement with amendment no 5. we would, however, suggest a slight stylistic correction: we think that the phrase should appear just after 'the commission' and not after the reference to the international monetary fund. in our view, it should therefore be worded as follows: 'the commission, in accordance with the principle of sound and efficient management, shall verify...' etc. written in this way, we would totally accept your proposal which would be written in a more correct way. amendment no 6 asks that the commission inform parliament before the second balance of payments credit payment is made. once again there is a problem between theory and practice. the problem is that decisions of this kind are often based on facts which i would not say should be secret but which should be treated with a certain degree of confidentiality, and therefore it would not be appropriate, in my view, to explain or comment on them in meetings which are quite public and well known. the problem is therefore how to ensure that parliament knows the facts before they happen, rather than learning them in a public sitting where we are discussing issues which i think could cause some problems. the formula i would now propose and which i could accept is that before any kind of payment is made, the chairmen of the committees concerned should be informed, for example, so that parliament is aware of it, without the duty of confidentiality which we must maintain in certain cases posing any problem. we fully agree with amendment no 7 concerning the sending of information before a specific date. finally, the amendment which is perhaps the most difficult from a conceptual point of view is the one concerning the additional 30 million in the form of a loan. the members are thus raising budgetary problems and, since this is a budgetary contribution, they will have to be solved. the formula proposed by the members to solve these problems is to include, already, an amendment to the financial perspective. i want to distinguish between two chronological processes. the year 1999 on the one hand and the year 2000 on the other. we are talking about 30 million euros, 15 for this year and 15 for next year. as regards the 15 million this year, i can already tell you that the figures are identified in the budget, so we have no difficulty in dealing with these payments immediately. on the other hand, as far as next year is concerned, we might have problems because in the commission's decision itself, as you can see on page 7, paragraph 4, it is clearly specified that, in view of the existing budgetary constraints, difficulties might arise and an adjustment might be necessary in category 4. so this problem is not new, of course, it is a problem that has been highlighted and i think you are right to raise it now. the difficulty is to know which solution we should look for. is there a specific solution to be introduced to change the financial perspective for a specific item by means of a specific council decision? we believe that this would raise problems of respect for budgetary authority and of fragmentation of the analysis of the subjects, which would prevent us from having a global view of the difficulties which might arise. i would therefore ask you not to include this reference to the amendment of the financial perspective, taking into account, of course, two things: firstly, it is obvious that in any case the commission will consider an amendment of the financial perspective in the future in which it will take account of this as well as other aspects related to kosovo; secondly, it is logically up to the budgetary authority, to you as parliament, to the commission in its proposals and to the council in its decisions, to take the appropriate decisions for the year 2000 without, however, prejudging the final solution at this time.
- the joint discussion is over. the vote will take place tomorrow at 12 noon. (the sitting was suspended at 1.20 p.m. and resumed at 3 p.m.)
mr president, i would like to say to my colleagues that once again i am afraid that our parliament is characterized by its modesty. mrs. rothe said 12% is not enough. mr langen said that the funds available were insufficient to cover 12% of production and we will, once again, despite these warnings, adopt this report. i think it is important to look at the question a little wider. it is not enough to look at the very low figures we are going to spend on renewable energy, perhaps we should also spend some time looking at the financial energies we are spending on other forms of energy, in particular nuclear energy. the green group's colleague recalled the tragedy of japan, but japan is not a country with problems, nor an underdeveloped country. so today we are going to spend, for the same period of the year, 60 million euros on fission, 15 million euros on renewable energy, and 200 million euros on fusion, and that gives us an idea of the real policies that the european union is pursuing. this is completely unacceptable. we cannot accept, especially since a number of member states have decided to break with nuclear policy, with fission policy, that the union as such continues to finance nuclear policy four times as much. we need to be aware of these differences. we must demand at least more money than we are going to spend on an energy we know nothing about, nuclear fusion, and we must immediately abolish the funds we are allocating to fission and transfer them entirely and directly to renewable energy. that's the point, i think. when today the major nuclear countries of europe, such as germany, are questioning their nuclear programme and france is starting to talk about it, we cannot continue to spend enormous sums, four times as much, i repeat, as we spend on renewable energies, on these energy policies. this is unacceptable. i therefore urge my colleagues to vote against this proposal for an agreement, especially since the council still allows itself to haggle over a few million. but of course that's not the problem. the problem is a funding problem. 12% is not enough, as mrs rothe said. we must set ourselves much more ambitious objectives and this parliament must oblige the council and the commission to find the means for this policy.
- (it) mr president, madam commissioner, i should like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, mrs ahern, for her work, which emphasizes the importance of promoting and improving energy efficiency. greater energy efficiency will mean, in the medium term, a reduction in consumption, the safeguarding of renewable energy resources, a reduction in our dependence on energy imports, and a reduction in environmental pollution. as regards the draft recommendation for second reading, i would like to make two comments and recall that the save programme is mainly aimed at strengthening research and the exchange of know-how and monitoring the results obtained in the sector (article 2), even if the financial envelope is limited. let me get to my point. firstly, as regards the proposal under consideration today, which aims to reduce final consumption by 1.5 points per year - amendment to recital 15 and article 1 - i think that the objective is certainly ambitious but unrealistic and harmful to users who have already successfully achieved, but not without great effort, the objectives set out in the save i programme (1991-95). in this connection, i would remind you that achieving energy efficiency in the short term comes at a cost to both companies and end consumers. therefore, targets for reducing final energy consumption should be carefully evaluated. we could risk burdening our productive apparatus with such high costs that they would make firms lose their competitiveness vis-à-vis other international competitors not subject to such constraints. i would like to make a few comments on the report by mr de gucht. i would remind you that promoting efficient energy consumption through extremely restrictive and perhaps unbearable legislative instruments and standards is not always effective. energy saving is a complex process of maturation, focused on the technological and economic awareness of companies and consumers. this is a long-term process. information campaigns to raise public awareness on energy-saving issues are welcome, but i do not see how the adoption of extremely binding legislative measures can be the most effective way of reducing final energy consumption. i refer here to amendment no 1, recital 11, the new recital 15a and amendment no 6 to article 1. conclusions: in the light of these considerations, i would like to draw your attention to the need, in such complex matters, in which the market and its rules are confronted with the collective welfare, to carry out analyses relating costs and benefits, with the long period of time for the introduction of measures to reduce final energy consumption as a reference point. such analyses are possible in transparent markets where each subject, the final consumer - company or consumer - can always assess how efficient energy consumption is through a process of technological and economic maturation, aided by efficient research and as complete information as possible.

commission - (it) mr helmer, i am very pleased that you are asking me these questions because, in fact, for many years my job has been to analyse competitiveness and the industrial economy. i fully agree with the points you have made: the reduction of corporate taxes and their harmonization are very useful for european competition, as is the tax on labour. if, on the other hand, you ask me what the first factor is for europe and its industrial problems, i will answer you in all sincerity: the problem of human resources as an incentive for entrepreneurship that our continent has and, therefore, the problem of preparing the financial structures for the new entrepreneurship. i therefore believe that the human factor is our main problem in productive development and that the tax factor is extremely important. we can give it the same importance as the other factor if you wish, but the problem of human resources is certainly, in my view, of paramount importance for our continent. if we do not urgently try to protect ourselves by means of a very large programme of action in this sector, i think that even the reduction of taxes - which has a very solid content and makes a very strong contribution - will not be enough to develop european industry.

- (it) mr president, in my view this debate and the motion we are discussing today are a good example of the desire of this parliament to send a clear, strong and friendly message of openness, a message which takes note of the new position of the greek government vis-à-vis the turkish government. however, we must also have the courage to admit that parliament now expects the political criteria - even before the economic criteria - of copenhagen to be met. the copenhagen political criteria are ethical criteria, they are the true identity of this parliament and of the european union. the european union is a state governed by the rule of law, and it bases its identity on this principle. this is a much more important aspect and a much more significant condition than being able to coin a common currency or have a common defence policy. ethical criteria are human rights, democracy, respect for minorities. i think that the summary we have made in our motion was necessary and we must submit it to turkey. the candidate status and the opening signals are not a problem, provided we have the courage to first of all say to ourselves that welcoming turkey into the european union will not be easy as long as the turkish government continues to ignore the security council resolutions on the invasion of cyprus, as long as it continues to deny the existence of a kurdish minority by generally calling them terrorists, as long as öcalan is threatened with the death penalty. i think we must go beyond a purely economic presentation and reading. we must demand concrete results in terms of democracy. we owe this guarantee not only to our treaties but above all to our peoples, and above all to the turkish people.

the rapporteur. - (it) madam president, it is perhaps too much to expect silence when one is speaking in a parliamentary assembly. a little whispering is enough. madam president, the report and the proposal for a decision which i am presenting to you and which i am briefly illustrating on behalf of the committee on constitutional affairs deal with the application of the interinstitutional agreement on the procedures for investigations relating to the fight against fraud. the president of parliament himself asked us to present this report. i would add that we are taking the opinion of the committee on budgetary control into account. i would like to remind you of the very simple facts. on 6 may 1999, the european parliament voted in plenary to adopt this interinstitutional agreement. on 25 may 1999, the president of parliament, mr gil-robles, the president of the council and the president of the commission signed the agreement. the council and the commission adopted the necessary decisions within their respective spheres of competence on 25 may and 2 june respectively. parliament is very late. the delay can of course be explained by the interruption caused by the elections and the opening of the new parliament. we must therefore act without further delay to avoid any misunderstanding about our willingness to cooperate fully with the more correct and rigorous conduct of investigations in the fight against fraud. the point is to introduce a new, very short, article 9c, in accordance with article 186c of the regulation. article 9c would make it possible to incorporate the text of the interinstitutional agreement as an annex, to which the necessary technical amendments have been made on the basis of the powers of our institution. i want to avoid any ambiguity and any ambiguity about the respective functions and competences of an institution such as the european parliament and an office such as olaf. in this connection, i would like to point out, madam president, that both recital 5 and the second paragraph of article 1 state very clearly that the provisions of the treaties in general and of the protocol on privileges and immunities in particular are intact and fully respected. i would add that during the debate in the committee on constitutional affairs on 14 october, some concern was expressed about article 2 in particular. all the members of the committee therefore agreed on an amendment which now appears at the end of article 2 as the last paragraph. the text of this amendment reads: 'this article shall apply without prejudice to the confidentiality requirements laid down by law, the treaties or the european parliament's rules of procedure.' i therefore believe, madam president, that this text can be voted on with complete confidence, especially since the committee on constitutional affairs has unanimously approved this report and this proposal for a decision.

commission. - mr president, i should first like to thank the rapporteur, mr harbour, for his in-depth analysis of the common position and for his very concise presentation of the subject under discussion today. the draft proposal for a directive aims to establish construction standards which manufacturers of fuel tanks will have to comply with in order to obtain ec typeapproval. the idea is, in particular, to introduce new provisions in council directive 70/221/eec on plastic fuel tanks for motor vehicles. the provisions will cover areas such as impact resistance, mechanical strength, fuel permeability and fire resistance. safety is of crucial importance when it comes to fuel tanks, especially when new materials and technologies are used. the draft proposal is directly in line with the technical requirements of regulation 34 of the united nations economic commission for europe which deals with fire prevention and more particularly the provisions concerning plastic tanks. the report proposes two amendments, both of which relate to the spill of diesel fuel which can be a serious danger to motorcyclists, especially in combination with wet roads, as mentioned. we fully agree that this is a serious problem from both a safety and environmental point of view and the commission can accept both amendments. in short, the commission can accept all the proposed amendments.
the rapporteur. - (it) mr president, this is a proposal for a regulation on technical matters, namely the obligation to install speed indicators on 'minor' vehicles, that is to say vehicles with two or three wheels, or even four in certain specific cases. this is still optional today and therefore provides an excuse - especially when it comes to vehicles which are manipulated in a certain way and manipulated in such a way as to allow the highest possible speed - for not respecting the speed regulations and therefore road safety, often causing serious accidents, especially involving young people. the proposal is therefore not strictly technical, but on the contrary, this small set of standards is becoming crucial in civil life. that is why we have put forward proposals and some amendments have been adopted, amendments which focus on the importance of road safety today, not only in the individual member states but also at community level. the delors white paper highlighted this crucial problem and expressed a firm commitment to reduce the number of road accidents, especially the most serious ones, within a few years. this is a moral commitment more than a legal one, a social commitment more than a technical one. we have followed this proposal very carefully. our committee has approved it and has wanted to stress the importance of not following a sectoral approach in the field of road safety, but a more complex and more unitary approach. we need a real package of road safety directives. the amsterdam treaty also makes road safety a community matter. the cross-interest of the car manufacturers gave rise to oppositions that were now defeated. finally, safety has become a fundamental element in the protection of human life and health. this set of regulations must undergo certain changes over time, due to technological innovations, innovations which serve to guarantee this more extensive protection. so how do we plan to change these regulations? this raises a problem which goes beyond the scope of this small proposal and involves a proposal of wider scope: the comitology, which mr harbour mentioned. this is a very important point and i would ask the commission and all members to consider it. our choice is rather innovative in that, by opting for an advisory committee rather than for regulation, we are aiming for a certain balance between the commission and the council. the approach adopted is common, it transcends individual states in a certain way without mortifying them, and it gives the commission a certain competence in matters of coordination and adjustment, in accordance with an approach which goes beyond individual realities which may sometimes oppose some resistance to such delicate aspects. after a first comparison, however, the commission did not seem so indifferent as to be in agreement with the council's ideas. it is clear, in fact, that when it comes to achieving some degree of regulatory compliance, each institution tries to bring the water to its own mill. however, in this case i have the impression - and i ask the commission to confirm this - that the commission and the council are leaning towards a regulatory committee. let's get this straight. i would remind you that this directive already provides for a deadline of 1 july 2001 for all vehicles and 1 july 2002 for mopeds - due to technical difficulties in adjustment - not to mention that we could end up with a conciliation procedure which could postpone the application of this directive indefinitely. the commission could commit itself to producing a transversal proposal, i.e. a more extensive one, which is not limited to this one proposal but which deals with a whole sector, for example the transport sector. the problem is not to extend it to a more general subject, which we shall have to deal with in any case, because the problem of comitology concerns many areas. a set of standards has already been repealed, so we can no longer refer to the old management or regulatory committees or the advisory committee. the commission could therefore adopt a more general position within a relatively short time. if necessary, we can abstain on this type of proposal. this is of course my personal view, which must also be assessed in conjunction with the legal affairs committee, because this objective would obviously be broader and therefore perhaps stronger than the more limited one. otherwise, everyone will go his own way and the institutions will make the decisions democratically.

the rapporteur. - (it) mr president, as everyone knows, the european coal and steel community was the first institutional nucleus around which the european edifice was gradually built. it has also made a significant contribution to the coal and steel industry system in these years, both in terms of social aid and research. the ecsc treaty is drawing ever closer to its expiry, which is set for 2002. parliament therefore has to make fundamental choices: how to manage the last phase of the treaty in terms of budgetary choices? will there be adequate attention to enterprises and workers in the sector, even after 2002? what liaison actions can be envisaged between the ecsc budget and the general budget of the union to facilitate the transition to the new procedures for access to finance for potential beneficiaries? the committee on budgets calls on parliament to give a clear answer to these three questions. it expects parliament to respect its prerogatives and to be prepared to encourage, through a dialogue which we hope will be productive with the commission and the council, the development of the experience gained so far with the treaty. the committee on budgets unanimously proposes the following - and i thank all my colleagues, from the president to all the representatives of the political groups, for the unanimous support they have given us -: 1. to restore the amounts in the 1999 budget; 2. allocate appropriations for the coal and steel sectors according to need, in particular by increasing social aid for coal and research funds for steel; 3. to request additional appropriations to finance measures to promote health and safety in mines in the coal sector and to ensure the development of clean technologies and high value-added products in the steel sector; 4. with a view to enlargement, to request that part of the phare programme be devoted to activities in the coal and steel sectors of the applicant countries; 5. strongly reiterate the call for reductions in administrative expenditure, which is still at the clearly disproportionate level of 19 years ago, and identify the real needs for human resources in relation to the union's current and future political priorities in this sector; 6. to insist that the management of the research fund for coal and steel, into which the interests of the reserves and provisions, as verified by the accounting officer, will be transferred, be entrusted to a specific body - agency or foundation - in accordance with the wish already expressed by parliament, in order to ensure a certain visibility of the actions in the coal and steel sectors and to guarantee the full participation of industry and workers in the use and programming of the research fund; 7. confirm that the transfer of the ecsc assets to the general budget will involve budgetary entry on revenue lines as well as on expenditure lines and will therefore require a technical adjustment of the current financial perspective; 8. the provide for pilot projects 'bridging' between the general budget and the ecsc budget with the aim of facilitating the transition between ecsc-funded activities and union programmes, in particular as regards the structural funds and research. the commission will have to attach particular importance to actions for which no adequate instrument has yet been provided at community level. on the basis of these general indications, mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to ask parliament to vote as uniformly and as broadly as possible. this would certainly strengthen our resolve.

europol in particular: because of its original characteristics and the fact that it escapes democratic and judicial control, this body continues, thanks to you, to absorb new competences. in this sense, europol has the characteristics of the phenomenon it wants to combat. as regards the measures to combat drugs for the period 2000-2004, the council now urges us to adopt this document without further delay. however, as the commission acknowledges, there has been no systematic and rigorous evaluation of the alleged results of the previous plan. the commission not only fails to say that the objective is far from being achieved, but it also notes an increase in drug trafficking and consumption, which means that a significant part of the resources spent so far have not been used to fight drugs but have contributed to their spread. what about liberties? - (it) madam president, the tampere summit finally agreed on the need to harmonize asylum and immigration policies, to strengthen investigative cooperation and to establish a european judicial area. this is what the alliance has been advocating in this house for at least eight years, and we are therefore pleased that the fifteen have reached a beginning of a common position. we hope that the speeches will give way to action. one thing is clear: there can be no social peace without security. one way to combat crime is to strictly control illegal immigration. immigration now affects all european countries. we must regulate and control it in all its aspects through common standards to avoid it leading to imbalances and tensions within our national societies. the creation of a european judicial area implies the elimination of inequalities between the legal systems of the member states and the modification, in this field, of the image of a europe at several speeds. a justice area will give form and substance to the third pillar and will enable us to fight more vigorously against organised crime, whatever its tragic nature: trafficking in women and children, paedophilia, prostitution of minors, production and trafficking of drugs, exploitation of prostitution, all fuelled in recent times by illegal immigration. ladies and gentlemen, i think it is hard to imagine that all this could happen in a relatively short time if we do not also send a clear message to the national states. in italy alone, in 1998, 130 000 criminal orders were issued, and the judges themselves say that the criminal procedure code is a failure and that the italian judicial system is bankrupt. we will only be able to contribute to europe's growth and prevent the spread of crime if we have common standards. to do this, we will also have to face another problem: how to prevent the application of safety standards from being uniform in all member states, given the opening of the frontiers?

and in any case the information to the trade union representatives of the workers - and the effective success, not only economic but also social, of the interventions, because the aid granted by the union to enterprises has not always led to the stable development of the activities of enterprises and the protection of their employment level. here it is appropriate to ask for a sanction to be applied which also includes the full reimbursement of the sums invested by the union itself. this principle must be applied even if the aid is intended for a non-community company. in this case, a cooperation agreement with the country where the company is located - - the next item is the recommendation for second reading (a5-0026/1999), on behalf of the committee on culture, youth, education, the media and sport, on the common position of the council with a view to adopting a european parliament and council decision establishing a single financing and programming instrument for cultural cooperation (culture 2000 programme) (13328/2/1998 - c5-0023/1999 - 1998/0169 (cod)) (rapporteur: mr graça moura).
- (it) mr president, we have succeeded in including explicit references to culture in the treaty; it's very important. we must not forget culture, the specific features and diversity of european culture, we must not forget to promote and disseminate it, now that we are in this delicate phase of the union, now that enlargement to the east is looming and all the economic and financial transformations linked to the advent of the single currency have begun. culture - which involves history, languages and traditions - must remain a fundamental and inalienable link for european citizens. we must therefore welcome the culture 2000 programme, but we must also repeat certain remarks which have already been made by most of my colleagues. firstly, european citizens, associations and small european businesses in the sector should be encouraged and stimulated to make the most of this programme and to avoid concentrating the already meagre resources on only a few major events, which would benefit a few privileged networks which, in the long run, as past experience has shown, end up with a monopoly of these investments. secondly, we should ensure that the financial envelope for this programme is increased. in conclusion, i believe that europe must not forget that by investing in culture we are not only preserving a great deal of heritage and our european identity, but also creating many jobs. i believe, mr president, that the citizens of europe are in great need of it at the moment.
commission. - mr president, first of all i would like to warmly congratulate your rapporteur on his excellent work and the committee on culture, youth, education, the media and sport on its support for the rapporteur. i would like to say how much the commission appreciates the fact that one of the great contemporary poets is the rapporteur for the culture 2000 programme. i would like to inform you, even if it is only an anecdote, that one of mr graça moura's books will be translated into swedish this year thanks to the ariane programme. this is to show you that european culture travels ! commitment of parliament to this programme and to european cultures. the culture 2000 programme is the first framework programme for culture. it will make it possible to develop a common cultural area designed to encourage creation, cooperation and exchanges; to safeguard and promote the heritage and history of the european peoples and to stimulate the flowering of cultures. you have stressed in your speeches and in our small talks in the corridors of parliament what is positive and what could be improved in the day-to-day management of this programme. i have noted the problems with the citizens, because you know that the conciliation procedure between parliament and the council must be launched quickly if, in 2000, citizens are to benefit from our culture 2000 programme. i know the expectations of parliament, and i must say very clearly that i share them. this is why most of the amendments tabled by parliament can be accepted by the commission. these are amendments nos 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 10, 12 and 14. i shall not repeat what is contained in these amendments, you know better than i do. i would just like to say that amendment no 4 on comitology is accepted in principle by a simple reference in the text to the management procedure provided for in the council decision of 28 june 1999 laying down the procedures for the exercise of implementing powers conferred on the commission. let me also make a comment on amendment no 12: this amendment is accepted while maintaining a certain flexibility in order to be able to reconcile the positions of the council and parliament on this specific point during the conciliation phase. i propose to accept amendments nos 7, 11 and 13 in part. amendment no 7, which concerns actions integrated into transnational cultural cooperation agreements, has two parts: firstly, the part concerning the role of the committee, and more particularly the obligation on the part of those responsible for the funded projects to submit a report of the actions undertaken directly to the committee, since this control is the responsibility of the commission, which must also assume responsibility for the management of the programme; secondly, the part which aims at adding the word 'heritage specialists'. the commission cannot accept this very generic term which would greatly and unpredictably expand the list of potential beneficiaries of the programme in terms of practical consequences. this does not mean, of course, that we do not accept the preservation of heritage, quite the contrary; the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used effectively. on the part of amendment no 7 accepted by the commission, i have only one comment concerning terminology. the term 'member or associated states' should be used, as it is a very modest correction. amendment no 11 on the information role of the contact points can be accepted provided that their regional actions, which can be exercised in addition to their national role, are based on the provisions in force in the individual member states and do not entail any increase in the overall financial contribution from the community. it is clear that the commission will discuss with the member states the breakdown of this aid between the national and the regional level. the commission accepts amendment no 13, which proposes indicative criteria for the distribution of the budget between the main cultural areas, while increasing the percentage for actions in the book and reading sector from 9% to 11%, the annual amount of which would be significantly reduced compared to the ariane programme. your rapporteur will certainly welcome this. i have heard all your demands on the budget. i am with you wholeheartedly, but there is realpolitik, and you know very well that the budgetary authority does not always share our views. i therefore feel obliged to maintain my initial position on amendment no 3 and cannot accept the proposal to increase the budget from 167 to 250 million euro. the three institutions have committed themselves to respecting budgetary planning and only the budgetary authorities, that is to say parliament and the council, can introduce an amendment. of course, if such an amendment were to be introduced, the commission would welcome it, but in the present state of affairs i am obliged to reject this amendment. mr rapporteur, ladies and gentlemen, i am glad that we can vote today on culture 2000. it will be a new starting point for our peoples who expect us to put people, culture, roots and the diversities that make europe so rich at the centre of our concerns, beyond economic activities. it is with this in mind and in the hope that we can move quickly in the direction our citizens want, that i ask you to vote on this report.

- the next item is the recommendation for second reading (a5-0038/1999) on behalf of the committee on culture, youth, education, the media and sport, on the common position adopted by the council with a view to adopting a european parliament and council decision establishing the community action programme 'youth' (13175/1/1999 - c5-0022/1999 - 1998/0197 (cod)) (rapporteur: mrs gröner).
- (it) mr president, the strengthening of the union must not be achieved solely through the euro. young people must feel part of a europe without frontiers, with a spirit that is more open and free. in short, they must acquire the adaptability and speed in cultural exchange, training and professional experience which are the most demanded elements in the society of the next millennium. the youth programme can help us to achieve these objectives. it can especially enable young people from less well-off and disadvantaged backgrounds to have transnational experiences which can be decisive for their positive integration into the world of work. as the rapporteur has stressed, the maximum dissemination of this programme is a priority and we must avoid that this kind of community action should remain the privilege of some because of a lack of information. i fully share the criticism of the council for refusing to grant the necessary budget increase to guarantee equal opportunities in access to the programme. it is also incredible that the protection of the rights of the participants, from the point of view of social security and medical assistance, cannot be guaranteed. in conclusion, i would like to stress this: in a society which is increasingly using telecommunications and the internet for communication, information and services, it is almost ridiculous that the council has rejected the amendments for the creation of a website entirely for young people.
- (it) mr president, commissioner, i was elected to the european parliament as leader of the italian pensioners' party. an hour ago, a member of my group said to me: "fatuzzo, did you hit your head somewhere, you, member of the party of pensioners talking about the youth program?" yes, it's true, two days ago i had a minor accident, but that's not why i'm talking about the youth program. i mention this because i want it to be known that in italy the party of pensioners and pensioners - and, in my opinion, all the elderly people in europe - approve of this programme for young people. they are in favour of young people getting to know each other, learning languages, moving from ireland to italy, from portugal to finland. this programme is of great importance because it brings europe closer to the young. i have tried to replace the word 'young' with 'old' in the programme in question, and the result is a very good project, which is also valid because it is true, as mrs taylor said, that these young people are the first generation to have a european identity and that, as mrs gröner said, the future of europe begins with young people. but do we ever think of those elderly people who built europe? well, i am going from approving the programme for young people to saying: let us build, let us build together also a programme for cultural exchanges between the elderly! maybe we won't spend a billion in seven years, given the age of the people involved, but seven billion in one year. it is not much, but it is certainly a positive element.
the rapporteur. - (it) mr president, i should like to say at the outset that, by letter of 6 july 1999, the council consulted parliament, in accordance with rule 157, on the proposal for a council decision on the community's participation in the european audiovisual observatory. at the sitting on 23 july the president of parliament proposed to the committee that i should chair a substantive examination, and at the meeting on 27 july the committee on culture and youth appointed me rapporteur. at its meetings on 22 september and 11 and 12 october the committee examined the proposal and the draft report and approved them unanimously. mr president, the european audiovisual observatory is a very important public-service body which is intended to collect and disseminate information on the audiovisual industry: cinema, television, video and so on. set up under the auspices of the 1989 eureka audiovisual programme, it operates under a partial agreement concluded within the council of europe and has as many as 34 european members and the commission of the european union as a whole among its members. as regards the network of partners, correspondents and other bodies, its mission should not be confused with the mission and duties of eurostat in this area - this is an important and fundamental element which the committee has already discussed at length. this is two different things: the community body is responsible for the harmonized and organic collection of general statistics from various member states, the public and the administrations are its main customers and therefore they are the ones who provide the work. the observatory has more specific, more targeted objectives, guided by the market and the statistical requirements of enterprises and professionals. in addition, its sphere of competence is wider: it covers the legal sector, specialised information, and its mandate, which therefore comprises 34 states - i emphasize this again - is broader than that of eurostat. we must not set these two institutions too much against each other, and we must not and cannot do so because they exchange regular contacts and have also established their modus operandi, they have reached an agreement on the way in which they will carry out their activities. on the other hand, the court of justice's judgment of 12 may 1998 on the legal bases required a more defined framework than in the past, precisely to avoid the confusion that existed previously. the new line concerns preparatory measures in the audiovisual sector. it makes no reference to the old article 130 (3) on industrial development and the decisions which led to the creation of an audiovisual eureka. a heading was provided for the establishment of a statistical information infrastructure. this structure should enable the community contribution to be paid to the budget of the european observatory in strasbourg for 1999, the current year. this payment will therefore be delayed, which has hampered and is still hampering the functioning of this institution. i believe - and the whole committee shares my view - that it is important and fundamental for the european commission and for the 34 member states which participate and continue to participate actively in the observatory that this institution functions. i also think we will have the opportunity to work with the excellent commissioner, mrs reding, on these matters, and i would like to welcome her and thank her for her availability. mr president, i have the honour of chairing the committee on culture and, of course, i am also responsible for expressing its position. as i said at the beginning, the proposal and the draft report were adopted unanimously in the final vote and as regards the three amendments in question, two were adopted unanimously and one was adopted by a majority. i would like to say, however, on a personal note that i was against the third amendment because the decision we are about to adopt, which the commission has already anticipated, was due to expire, in the original text, on the last day of the last month of the fifth year, whereas the commission has reduced this deadline to three years. i repeat that, on a personal level, i remain perplexed because i believe that a distinction is being made between eurostat and the observatory, which perhaps does not favour collaboration between these two institutions, collaboration which is not unheard of at the moment. mr perry, who signed the amendment, was rightly concerned that parliament would have to control the observatory's expenditure and budget during these years. he can rest assured that we have extended the deadline to five years, so that the observatory can plan its valuable functions in a more extensive and more organic way.
- (it) mr gargani, the italian radicals would have liked to amend, to participate, to have the opportunity to intervene in this report as in all the others. that is impossible for them because, as mrs bonino said this morning, we are not allowed to carry out this parliamentary activity which is a characteristic of the european parliamentary mandate. we have therefore not tabled, or rather have not been able to table, amendments and we will not be voting on this report. we regret this, because this is an important matter. in addition, our experience as an audiovisual observatory is significant in italy. it was we radicals who, perhaps the first, independently created an audiovisual listening centre responsible for the control of information, for example political, in the various programmes, the various television news, the media. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one. this control would have been part of the observatory's tasks, in view of european integration which must increasingly become political integration, integration of societies and the way of making policy, integration of information at european level. i put this idea to you, mr president gargani, because i myself am unable to translate it into a parliamentary act, as we are not allowed to do so in the present state of affairs.
the rapporteur. - (it) mr president, the idea of including turkey among the beneficiary countries of the youth for europe and socrates programmes dates back to 1996. the commission's proposal dates from 14 may 1996 and is in line with the objective of extending the vast cultural market to this country as well. this proposal was put forward despite the obstacles which existed, namely the human rights situation in turkey and the cyprus problem. despite these difficulties and the recent increase in problems, this idea led parliament, at its sitting on 25 february, to extend this decision to turkey. the commission has also decided to do so in the last few days. ladies and gentlemen, i think this is a very important decision. why would you do that ? it is true that turkey has raised some problems in this respect and i admit, mr president, that the echo of these difficulties has reached the ears of the committee. it has indeed been difficult to get this idea accepted in the european context, in a europe so open to culture, as was repeated this afternoon by the many speakers who dealt with the four provisions, which are in some ways related. but i think that this decision defines europe as a group of states which are rather open to new countries, and with great dignity; the european union is a great example of this, and it offers great opportunities for freedom and cultural development. in my view, when parliament approves this kind of provision, it opens up a great opportunity for young turks who, renouncing the mistakes of their fathers, can thus see europe as a reality offering great freedom, as a union of states in which culture, its dissemination and solidarity between young people are important and fundamental elements. i ask you to approve this provision unanimously, as was the case in committee. i hope that, despite the delay in the 1999 programmes, it will be implemented in time for the year 2000, which is fast approaching.
- (it) mr president, madam commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the italian delegation of the national alliance is clearly in favour of turkey being included among the beneficiary countries of the youth for europe and socrates programmes. we are not only paying a legitimate tribute to culture, but we are also making a calm political judgment which is the result, above all, of the awareness of having as a partner a state - turkey, precisely - which is still far from fully respecting human rights and the principles of freedom and democracy, the basic principles of the european union. that is precisely why we must stimulate and encourage the full participation of young people in this country in a valid policy of exchange, mobility and encounter with young europeans. the young have always been the first to grasp the demands of a people for novelty, change, growth and cultural change. the young people are a very effective instrument for transmitting sound, democratic values. the european parliament was therefore right to vote in favour of turkey's participation in the exchanges provided for in the two programmes at first reading - and i thank the rapporteur, mr gargani, for his support. moreover, to deny turkey this possibility would be to increase the isolation of this mediterranean country from a europe which must seek dialogue, especially with those who - young people, in this case - cannot be held responsible for the mistakes of a government which we still have reservations about. allowing turkey to participate in the youth for europe and socrates programmes does not mean giving legitimacy to a state which has not yet shown tangible proof of maturity in respecting the rights of minorities, but it does allow these young people to stimulate the slow and painful process.

in writing. - (it) the commission waited until the last minute to ask for negotiations to be opened. this is very regrettable, especially in view of the overall development of the whole matter, which, already in previous years, had seen a conflict arise in the institutional framework of the consultation, in relation to the role of parliament in an agreement with important budgetary implications. the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on fisheries, which are not in line with the commission's proposals. no one wants to question the effectiveness of cooperation instruments with morocco through negotiations which, as soon as possible, promote the interests of both community and moroccan operators, and thus underline the feeling of solidarity which, moreover, even in sectors other than purely economic ones, has been manifested above all between the countries of southern europe, in the mediterranean region. the european fleet is very careful to respect the specific environmental compatibility standards in fishing - biological rest period, fishing framework, prohibition of fishing with trawlers, etc. however, it requires compensatory socioeconomic measures following an agreement further restricting its activities, including those of the processing industry. let us be clear: compensation cannot be at the expense of the agricultural sector, which has already suffered many budget cuts. the new fifg regulation gives us reassurance in this respect, because it covers not only the effects of international agreements but also the unforeseen events in the fisheries sector. finally, a word on the role of parliament: members of parliament will have to act as observers in all negotiating procedures and will have to look after the interests of the european fishing fleet.
mr president, the french embargo on british meat raises, in my view, two types of problem, both of considerable importance and significance for the future of europe. on the one hand, we must make the very existence of the european union a reality and thus ensure that decisions taken by the competent bodies on matters of community importance are applied in all the member states, especially in cases such as the one we are discussing today, which may invalidate one of the fundamental conditions of the union, namely the free movement of goods. on the other hand, we must protect consumers, and this is an absolute priority whose scope is so wide that it also justifies national choices which are not in accordance with community decisions, provided that these choices can be explained from a scientific and health point of view. the evaluation of these two types of problems cannot be limited to specific positions taken in parliament in support of or against the french decision. in fact, it is for other institutional bodies to assess the validity of the legal conditions and, above all, the health grounds put forward by the french authorities. in this connection, we look forward to commissioner byrne's press conference this afternoon. parliament would be wrong to hold a debate on this, a debate which would involve formulating a political judgment on the conduct of anyone; the commission has also proposed that the council should be consulted on the draft budget. it seems appropriate and desirable, however, to take advantage of these opportunities to resume the work of weaving together the community institutions, which this parliament must undertake in accordance with the mandate of all the europeans it represents, by integrating the role hitherto played by national governments in community construction. in addition, this parliamentary body must act as a counterweight to the executive, and therefore to the commission, and must assume this role with greater vigour. this is a feature of all modern democracies. let us return to the case in question. i do not think that parliament should ask the commission to investigate the existence of recommendations issued by national agencies responsible for food safety which would run counter to community decisions. the commission and other relevant community institutions should be asked to draw up a report on the implications of these recommendations, so that parliament can make valid criticisms in preparation for the improvement of existing community legislation in this field.
- (it) mr president-in-office of the council, commissioner, i think that if the millions of unemployed in europe had heard this first report on the macroeconomic dialogue to promote growth and employment, they would have pulled their hair out. i understood that beyond a few statements of principle there is nothing incisive, there is no proposal, there is no rigorous analysis, as should be the analysis of the state of health of many european economies, especially continental ones. commissioner, when i hear the word 'macro-economic dialogue' - and i admit that the definition seems to me to be at least elusive - i think of the consultation in italy. what i fear is that this macroeconomic dialogue will attempt to export to the european level the method and mechanism of concertation followed in italy, a method which has led our country to the lowest growth rates and the highest unemployment and inflation rates. the consultation between the social partners and the government in italy has become a pact between representatives of the vested interests - trade unions, industry, politics - who aim to defend vested interests and profits against the possibility of making a place for the unemployed and all those who are not represented in the consultation in the new economy. in italy, consultation with the social partners has blocked all strategies and reform assumptions on the crucial points. i am thinking of the labour market, public spending and social security in particular; the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat unemployment. both the council representative and the commission representative were specific on only one point: the request for wage moderation. in italy, wage moderation, which was agreed by the oligarchic trade unions, has blocked any plan for economic reform. i wonder why we have to tell european workers that the only thing that is certain is that they will be paid moderately. why should we not instead tell them that if, thanks to liberalisation and our ability to accept the challenges of the new economy openly, we manage to reverse the downward trend in economic growth, we will benefit from a certain amount of wealth and it will even be possible to raise wages, possibly by linking them to productivity and company results rather than to national or european collective agreements. in conclusion, i think that this dialogue should perhaps serve to explain to the european citizens why employment in the united states has increased by 45 per cent in 20 years, while in europe it has increased by only 4.12 per cent. i believe that the unemployed in europe, and perhaps even immigrants, need liberalisation, less state control, less taxation and less economic management. this is likely to lead to economic growth and new jobs.

- the next item is the report (a5-0047/1999) by mr linkohr, on behalf of the committee on industry, foreign trade, research and energy, on the proposal for a council decision on the conclusion of the agreement for scientific and technical cooperation between the european community and the argentine republic (com (1999) 292 - c5-0040/1999 - 1999/0125 (cns)).

the directive, in its current form, was only a first step towards better consumer information. i will give two examples: firstly, we intend to develop this directive further as soon as possible, applying article 9 to the question of the verification procedure and article 10 to the question of the establishment of the committee. commission officials are currently working on the application of these articles. it is more than likely that we will be returning to some of the comments made by the committee on the environment in the course of this work. the commission also plans to create a web page containing information on fuel consumption and carbon dioxide emissions from cars sold in the european union. this shows that we want consumers to be informed as soon as possible. i would like to say to mr lange - and this is not the first time we have discussed this - that i take this matter very seriously. we will have to return to the question of how the european parliament should be involved and kept informed of the work on voluntary agreements. i would remind you that parliament, the council and the meeting of european car manufacturers have agreed on the urgent need to conclude agreements with manufacturers who are not members of such associations at european level. but, as i have said, i take the common position adopted by the european parliament seriously. i would also like to say that i do not intend to initiate any new projects in the field of environmental protection until the question of the role of the european parliament has been resolved. in conclusion, i would like to say that the commission is pleased that the common position has been so well received by the rapporteur and the committee on the environment. we hope that the plenary will follow the recommendations of the rapporteur.
- thank you, commissioner. the debate is closed. the vote will take place tomorrow at 11 a.m.
- the next item is the oral question on behalf of the committee on legal affairs and the internal market on the communication from the commission to the european parliament and the council entitled 'strategy for the european internal market' (b5-0032/1999)
- (it) mr president, i too thank the commission. the strategy paper which has been submitted to us certainly represents an important step towards the single market and extends and consolidates the idea of the single market which is one of the central pillars of european construction. it suggests a series of scenarios in which the single market is also likely to bring new freedoms and opportunities for the ordinary citizen. i do not wish to dwell on certain legal matters which have already been well dealt with by mr lehne and mrs palacio; it seems to me more interesting to dwell on some political subjects which are not yet fully covered by the document but which will certainly be discussed in this parliament, because they strengthen the idea of europe. firstly, how can the single market be made effective where states are trying to introduce protectionist measures - we have seen several cases of this recently - and how will the commission make the best use of the infringement procedure by making it faster and by encouraging citizens and their associations to denounce the violations of community law which take place every day in the various member states? a second topic is the use of new technologies. the european union must provide a direct legal framework to enable free movement and the free provision of services and to avoid conflicts and obstacles to free movement caused by too disparate legislation between the different countries of the community. the directive on electronic signatures will no doubt be followed by those on electronic commerce and copyright in the information society, on consumer protection in the distance sale of financial and other products. all the european institutions will have to ensure that the coherence of the common legal institutions is guaranteed. in these areas, a fair balance between public regulation and self-regulation by the parties concerned will allow the market to function without infringing the rights of citizens and consumers. on the other hand, these new technologies require consumer confidence to be fully exploited. giving out your credit card number should not and cannot be a risk. we have a third subject: how does the commission intend to proceed with the gradual extension of the single market to the applicant countries? this must be done without calling into question the community acquis and ensuring the balance, however fragile, that currently exists. the national markets of the applicant countries will have to be integrated into the community market without any clashes, and it will therefore be necessary to provide for transitional measures where some countries are not yet ready; the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. one last point: what does this document say about international economic relations, which will be of vital and brief importance in the millennium round? we are living in an unprecedented historical phase which requires a great capacity to adapt to change, to prevent the european model from losing out on the international scene. we live in an age of interdependence: different aspects of economic and social life are intertwined at a global level and everyone's interests are intertwined. interdependence requires that the transition from diversity to uniformity be accommodated wherever possible and managed in such a way as to maintain a balance between identity, diversity and the conviviality of the models. the inevitable confrontation with diversity implies that one must be able to adapt one's own identity to the level necessary to be able to compete on equal terms with others. finally, in the sectors concerned by international multilateral trade, we are seeing how the mutual influence between organizations at the global level is becoming increasingly greater every day. we know that the european model is already being used as the basis for the transatlantic dialogue. we must prevent our efforts from implying that others impose models on us that are not our own.

- (it) mr president, i refer to rule 43, annex 2, concerning the time limits for the transmission of questions. i have already raised the matter and the president has graciously sent me a reply which i do not think is in accordance with the rules of procedure because if the rules of procedure say a week, it cannot be seven working days: seven days may correspond to seven calendar or working days but certainly not to a week. the same applies to a month or a year which cannot, for example, be equal to 365 working days. moreover, the time limit set for the thursday of the week preceding that in which the question is debated, since the questions are examined on tuesday, is actually eight working days, which is beyond interpretation. in my case, the matter would have been considered on wednesday, which would have been nine working days. in any event, it is not the bureau - a meeting of 22 and 23 november 1979 is cited here - or the bureau acting on a proposal from the president, in agreement with the chairmen of the political groups, in a decision of 19 november 1992, which can interpret the rules of procedure. only one body within this parliament can decide on the interpretation of the rules of procedure, namely the committee on constitutional affairs. i therefore ask, in accordance with the rules of procedure, that the matter be referred back to the competent body.
