- (it) mr president, i voted in favour of the murphy report. i think it is quite right that the european parliament is trying to avoid delays in the payment of commercial transactions. as a representative of the pensioners' party, i hope that one day parliament will also take an interest in the public administrations which have to pay pensions to the beneficiaries of the 15 member states. in italy, for example, it takes 10, 15, or even 20 years for an elderly person, even a 90-year-old, to receive what he is owed. i would not mind if the public administration in the 15 member states were given the same attention as individuals.
- (it) mr president, i am particularly pleased that the provisions on the labelling and identification of cattle have been adopted, but i would like to see people, human beings, labelled too, especially pensioners. i would like to know when they were born, how they were made because -- and i notice this a lot -- they're considered numbers. yes, the bureaucracy considers old people who apply for a pension to be numbers - i recall that i represent the party of pensioners and have observed this fact on many occasions - and that is why i would like to see the photographs of these citizens attached to the applications, so that they are labelled and treated at least in the same way as cattle.
mr rothley. - (de) i am not sure that the commissioner has been able to answer the question. i would like to stress first of all that the commission started to deal with the crossborder system of book prices in germany and austria long before the austrian company libro denounced the case in 1996. the proceedings were initiated in 1993 after german and austrian publishers had denounced these crossborder agreements to the commission. this was followed in 1994, 1995 and 1996 by denunciations by two german booksellers and the austrian federal chamber of labour, an organisation which also plays the role of consumer advocate. the commission has not yet completed its examination of the cases in question and therefore cannot anticipate what the eventual decision will be as a result of the proceedings currently under way. it is the duty of the commission not to discuss, except with the persons concerned, an individual case of competition still under consideration, but it can provide a response to the general aspects raised in the oral question, which i am happy to do before this house. national price systems for books may be based on national legislation or on agreements between companies, in this case between publishers and booksellers. it is impossible to compare such legislation exactly with an agreement between undertakings. different provisions of the treaty apply to them. legislation-based systems must be examined on the basis of article 28 of the treaty, while agreements between undertakings must be compatible with article 81 of the treaty. in both cases, the case law of the court of justice must of course be respected. the commission considers that national price systems for books, based on agreements between undertakings, are compatible with community competition rules if they do not significantly interfere with trade between member states. in such cases, article 81 shall not apply. as regards systems based on legislation, each member state is free to establish a legislative framework provided that it complies with the principle of the free movement of goods enshrined in the treaty. this choice is left to the national authorities. as regards more particularly the french legislation - to which mr rothley referred - as it was drafted at the time, the judgment of the court of justice in case 229/83 leclerc v au blé vert of 10 january 1995 stated that, in the context of national legislation establishing a fixed price system for books, rules requiring compliance with the sales price fixed by the publisher in the sale of books published in one member state and reimported into that member state after export to another member state, unless there are good reasons to believe that the books in question were exported for the sole purpose of reimportation with a view to circumventing the legislation, constitute measures equivalent to import restrictions, prohibited by the former article 28 (article 30) of the treaty. finally, i would point out that such legislation in germany and austria would not contribute to the harmonization of european law. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the price of books is not increased by the same amount as in the case of books sold in the member states.
- (de) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the question of the fixed price system for books is a question of cultural policy par excellence. the very fact that i am addressing you here, mr president, commissioner, in german shows that a fixed price system for books is not just a national problem, but a problem that goes beyond national borders and concerns a number of small linguistic islands outside germany and austria. the book is a cultural asset, writing the first transcribed form of language, of linguistic identification. and while it is very important to respect the rules of competition, the question of the cultural dimension must be equally important. if the legal possibilities we have at present within the european union do not yet allow us to reach crossborder rules in this area, i hope that this will be the case in the near future, especially after the austrian and german ministers of culture in february last year recognised the need for crossborder rules in this area and provided a written argument on this. in order to protect authors and publishers, i think it is necessary to find a regulation on this matter. if it is no longer in the form of planned crossborder measures, it will have to be done at national level. the problem of importation, whether contractual or legislative, is a fundamental point in this connection. i hope that the rapprochement between the commission and parliament, which has already raised this issue on many occasions in the previous parliament, both in the committee on culture and in the plenary, will continue. and to quote mr rothley: there can be no question of continuing to have a variety of disparate national legislation in this area in the future. we need sensible regulation, which will not be optimal, but which will nevertheless meet the expectations of the professional organisations concerned. the french model could in the near future offer us a useful and viable solution. in this respect, we hope, commissioner, that you will be able to give effect to the wishes expressed by parliament here and in the documents of this parliament. (de) mr president, i am glad that we can say that the death penalty has been abolished in europe. the abolition of the death penalty is almost part of our culture. unfortunately, it was not possible to incorporate abolition into un texts in 1999. we all know that the death penalty is still on the agenda in iran, turkey, china and yemen. i find it particularly tragic that the death penalty is still maintained in america, in the country which has set itself the goal of freedom and human rights. at the moment, two eu citizens are awaiting the death penalty, a spaniard in yemen and an italian in the united states. the latter, mr barnabei, is to be executed in the year 2000. mr. barnabei, a formerly upright man, is accused of murdering a young woman. this concerns me all the more as he is an italian citizen, like me. since leading american jurists say that this could be a miscarriage of justice, which happens quite often, but also because he is an eu citizen, i ask the commission and of course this parliament to do everything possible to commute this death sentence into a life sentence or to defer it.
- (it) mr president, for a christian, the demand for a moratorium on the death penalty and its abolition is not only a duty or a due act, but a moral choice of respect for a person who has failed and who can, and must, redeem himself. we are opposed to all deaths caused by human hands, such as abortion and euthanasia. the motion ignores the judgment of guilt. we would ask for a moratorium again even if the convict had confessed and we do so all the more so when there may be serious doubt as to his guilt. if we demand, on the basis of official human rights documents, humane conditions in the prison universe, we are even more opposed to the death penalty and therefore cannot understand how supposedly civilized countries can maintain barbaric laws. finally, i would like to emphasize that, in the modern conception of the study of the consequences of criminal judgment, punishment must have a re-educational function enabling the rehabilitation of the bankrupt citizen and his reintegration into civil society. we are aware of these values and we are reiterating our strong call for a moratorium on the death penalty for the two citizens convicted in virginia and yemen.
- (it) mr president, we are not invoking an abstract principle today, but we are intervening on behalf of a 33-year-old italian-american, rocco derek barnabei, who has been living for six years on death row in a prison in virginia, with the daily threat of death for a crime he most likely did not commit. how many other people are in the same situation as him? how many other people don't have the funds to pay for lawyers or expensive tests that could possibly exonerate them completely? it is our duty to condemn the death penalty without mercy and to continue to call, as the pope did last sunday, for at least the moratorium proposed by italy to the un member countries to be applied. we hope that the third millennium will begin with the acceptance of this proposal. in the meantime, we will continue to work concretely, supporting initiatives such as that of the tuscany region to raise funds to enable rocco barnabei to pass the necessary dna tests to prove his innocence. we call on you, mr president, to use all the means at your disposal to make the voice of parliament and the european institutions heard by the us authorities.
- (it) mr president, an extraordinary 21-year-old afghan girl, who miraculously escaped the barbarism of the taliban and is now a refugee with hundreds of thousands of women in islamabad, said to me: 'now that you are in the european parliament, speak about us! here, take my burka. show it, show the world the symbol and reality of our denied existence, imprisoned, our bodies raped, killed, stoned". that is why today i will be wearing a burqa to speak before this parliament. i make this gesture on behalf of all the women who are murdered, raped and denied their existence in afghanistan and around the world. (applause) every moment, women's rights are trampled on in afghanistan. afghan women and their resistance need our solidarity and commitment. for this reason, we call on parliament, the council, the commission and the united nations not to recognise the taliban regime and to promote an action plan for ngos working in afghanistan to ensure that the fundamental freedoms of afghan women are respected with a view to their economic and social emancipation, and for ngos helping afghan refugee women. we must also do something else together: grant asylum and assistance to women who have been forced to flee afghanistan. - (it) mr president, as the applause for mrs redondo jiménez has confirmed, this is one of the most important measures we have voted for. i think that statistics are fundamental and italy is in the most absolute chaos when it comes to the milk produced by its cows. i therefore voted in favour of the report and wish to express the wish that statistics should also be drawn up on the number of pensioners in the 15 member states. many people say that there are too many of them, and i think the opposite: the statistics will enable us to verify this. i would like to see more of them, because retirement is a beautiful thing.
- (it) mr president, before i left for strasbourg, the pensioners who accompanied me to the airport asked me if we would talk about beer on friday morning. i said, "i'm sure you would". they said: "you have to explain why you voted and say that the pensioners are in favour of the production and development of beer". we are in favour of this not only because the party of pensioners, ten years ago, put forward as a candidate for mayor of the city of rome the top model solveig tubing, born in berlin, a great lover and expert of beer, but also because the statistics i have compiled on the subject have shown that beer rejuvenates those who drink it. i know that the social security agencies and governments are opposed to the development of beer because they will have to pay pensions longer, but as a representative of the party of pensioners i am in favour of it.
- (it) mr president, i should like to say how pleased i am to make this last explanation of vote in 1999 in favour of the savary report, which i voted for. i am very much in favour of this initiative by the european community to provide practical aid to the most deserving regions, such as tajikistan. as mr blokland has said in his statement, i would like to say to commissioner wallström - who is disturbing the sleep of italian, greek and spanish pensioners with old cars which must be quickly taken off the road - that i would be grateful if the aid granted to tajikistan and other countries were also controlled as to its allocation. i would be glad if they were also given to people who need them, such as pensioners.
